# Implemented Specs

Record of specs under `specs/` that have been implemented. This file is the
**source of truth** for the `implement-spec` skill: a spec is considered done
**iff** it has a `## <name>` heading below whose text matches the spec's
filename without the `.md` suffix.

Entries are newest first. Do not delete history; append or update in place.

**The `Commit:` field.** It holds a commit hash, `uncommitted`, or
`committed (hash not recorded)` — nothing else (`tests/test_spec_ledger_invariants.py`
enforces this). An entry written before its commit exists records `uncommitted`, and the
next `implement-spec` run fills the hash in; leaving it is how the field went stale on 66
entries at once. Two things a reader should know about the hashes now recorded:

- **67 fields were backfilled on 2026-07-29** (`deep-dive-audit-2026-07-29-minor-fixes`,
  item 3) from a **derived** rule, not from a record kept at the time: the commit that
  added that entry's `## <name>` heading to this file, which in this repo is the commit
  carrying the implementation (spot-checked, and confirmed exactly where two headings
  share a prefix). Derived, but verified to resolve.
- **17 hashes recorded by hand before that no longer resolved** in the current history
  (e.g. `d69c360`, `0cf7e9f`, `9391bf1`) — every one dated 2026-07-15 or 2026-07-16, orphaned
  by a history rewrite in that window. **Repaired 2026-07-31** across the 22 entries carrying
  them, to the surviving commits, recorded as `<new>` (was `<old>`, pre-2026-07-16 history
  rewrite) so the rewrite stays visible and an old note is still resolvable by hand. Each
  mapping was established two independent ways that never disagreed: the surviving commit's
  subject is the exact `#1 <spec-name>`, and/or it is the commit that added the entry's
  `## <name>` heading here. `tests/test_spec_ledger_invariants.py` now asserts every recorded
  hash **resolves**, which the vocabulary check never did — it accepted any 7–40 hex run, so
  hash-*shaped* passed for hash-*valid* and 22 entries answered with a hash identifying
  nothing while looking answered.

- **MCP re-check:** <server version + date + outcome, or "n/a (no Senzing fact)">

## mapping-workflow-tells-the-guide-not-to-ask-and-the-plugin-never-reconciles-it

- **Implemented:** 2026-08-12
- **Files changed:** `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/module-05-data-quality-mapping/phase2-data-mapping.md` (new "The tool's responses instruct *you*" section), `tests/test_tool_directives_do_not_override_interaction.py` (new, 8 tests), `specs/INVARIANTS.md` (**INV-205** + index entry), `tests/test_invariant_enforcer_citations.py` (`EXPECTED_PAIRS` 22 → 23), `specs/mapping-workflow-tells-the-guide-not-to-ask-and-the-plugin-never-reconciles-it.md` (deviation + invariant notes), `specs/IMPLEMENTED.md` (this entry)
- **MCP re-check:** **server 1.32.9**, 2026-08-12, `get_capabilities` re-called this session. The two quoted directives were captured from **live `mapping_workflow` responses earlier in the same session** (`action='start'` and the first `advance`), not carried out of the spec. `mapping_workflow` was deliberately **not** re-called during implementation — it would start a fresh workflow to re-read strings already obtained live today, and this change asserts no Senzing fact.
- **Summary:** The bootcamp's interaction rules now outrank conversational directives embedded in a tool response. **Criterion walk, 8 of 8 hold, all runtime-verified.** (1) *States the precedence and quotes the directives* — the new section quotes *"Do NOT ask the user to confirm, approve, type YES, or proceed. Do NOT wait for a response. Just advance."* and *"MAPPER LANGUAGE — determine from context (do not ask)"*, both with server and date. (2) *Guided-vs-faster behaviour at the entity-plan advance* — guided ends the turn on the module's own 👉 before advancing; faster advances in the same turn, which is what the tool wanted. (3) *Scope limit explicit* — payload shape, advance schema, opaque state, resource locations and Senzing facts stay tool-authoritative (INV-080), with the memorable form *"'Do not ask the user' is ours to override; '`profile_summary` is an array' is not."* (4) *The pinned verbosity question is unchanged* — verified by `git diff` and pinned by its own assertion. (5) *A test asserts the statement, non-vacuously* — 8 tests over three classes; it names the file, requires >20,000 chars, and fails if the section is deleted. (6) *Negative-controlled, both mutations verified to land* — deleting the whole section fails **4** tests; deleting **only** the scope limit fails **2**, which is the one that matters, since an unscoped "ignore the tool" would be a worse defect than the one being fixed. Both reverted from `cp` backups. (7) *Full suite* — **1767 passed, 3 skipped** (was 1756; +11 across both specs this run). (8) *Stdlib-only* — `re`, `unittest`, `pathlib`; no `plugins/` import. **Establishes INV-205** (maintainer-approved wording, sign-off taken before writing): a conversational directive inside an MCP tool response MUST NOT override the bootcamp's interaction rules, scoped to conversation. Index entry added under **MCP sourcing and tool contracts** in the same edit, and the rule-3 binding was **mutation-tested** — removing INV-205 from the index fails `test_invariants_index.py` with `[] != [205]`. **Deviations recorded:** the guard is broader than the criteria asked for (8 tests rather than one presence check, including the scope class), disclosed rather than folded in; and the MCP re-verification is precise about what was re-called versus what was captured live earlier in the session. **⚠️ Caught by this repo's own guard, worth recording:** the first full-suite run after adding INV-205 **failed twice** — `test_invariant_enforcer_citations.py` flagged that INV-205 named an enforcer which did not cite it back, and that the pinned pair count had moved 22 → 23. That guard was written **this morning**, in `the-invariant-to-enforcing-test-link-is-asserted-nowhere`, for exactly this defect; it caught its author creating it within hours. Both fixed (citation added, count re-derived by running the extractor rather than incremented to pass).
- **Commit:** `b78135e`

## license-request-omits-a-required-field-the-server-demands

- **Implemented:** 2026-08-12
- **Files changed:** `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/module-04-data-collection/SKILL.md` (field list + ⛔ note + the pinned consent question), `tests/test_mcp_call_contracts.py` (`license_window()` helper + 3 new tests), `specs/license-request-omits-a-required-field-the-server-demands.md` (deviation note), `specs/IMPLEMENTED.md` (this entry)
- **MCP re-check:** **confirmed, server 1.32.9, 2026-08-12**, re-asked this session before any edit. `get_capabilities`' manifest lists *"firstname (required), lastname (optional), email (work email required — personal domains rejected), and how_heard"*, and the `submit_feedback` schema's own property descriptions read `how_heard` *"(required for license_request)"* against `lastname` *"(optional for license_request)"*. Tools called: `get_capabilities`; `submit_feedback` schema loaded via `ToolSearch`. ⛔ **`submit_feedback` was never invoked** — verified by `git diff`, which adds no `submit_feedback(` call.
- **Summary:** The eval-licence step now names all three required fields and the consent question states the real payload. **Criterion walk, 9 items: 8 hold, 1 disclosed as not runtime-verifiable.** (1) *The step states first name, work email and how they heard, with only `lastname` optional* — rewritten as "**requires three values**", verified by opening the file. (2) *Provenance inline* — a ⛔ note carries both sources, server 1.32.9 and the date, scoped to that paragraph, and explains **why** the schema misleads: `submit_feedback` has no `required` array at all, so a caller checking only what the schema marks required omits the field (the INV-192 class). (3) *The pinned consent question matches* — now "including your name, work email, and how you heard about us"; the pre-existing guard matches on the question's prefix and still passes untouched, verified rather than assumed. (4) *The licence tests assert the field list, not only the gate* — 3 new tests. (5) *Negative-controlled, both mutations verified to land* — reverting the prose to "optionally a last name and how they heard" fails `check='not grouped with the optional field'`; stripping "how you heard" from the consent question fails `states='heard'`. Reverted from a `cp` backup. (6) *The `CONDITIONALLY_REQUIRED` comment and the shipped text agree* — asserted in both directions, so they cannot drift apart again. (7) *No `submit_feedback` call added* — `git diff` grep returns zero. (8) *Full suite* — **1767 passed, 3 skipped**. (9) *Stdlib-only, cross-platform* — `re`, `unittest`, `pathlib`. **⚠️ Not runtime-verified, and cannot be here:** that the server *rejects* a `license_request` missing `how_heard` is established from its schema and manifest, never observed — confirming it would mean sending a real name and work email upstream, which the dry-run rule forbids. The documentation discrepancy is proven; the runtime consequence is not. **Deviations recorded — the guard took three attempts and both failures were caught only by running the mutation:** the first version asserted the *token* `how_heard` appeared and passed on a reverted file, because the token survived in the quoted `get_capabilities` manifest added as provenance; widening to "no 'optional' near 'heard'" then failed on the **correct** tree, for the same reason inverted, since that manifest legitimately reads "lastname (optional), … and how_heard". Resolved by asserting the claim where the claim is made — the field-list statement cut at the following ⛔ — plus an assertion that the statement still precedes that ⛔ so the scoping cannot silently drift. **Establishes no invariant** — INV-192 and INV-135 already govern; this is an unapplied instance of both.
- **Commit:** `b78135e`

## dry-run-2026-08-12c

**Not a spec** — a dated record of a dry-run, following the `dry-run-*` precedent. **Phase 3 only**; phases 1–2 of the same session are recorded separately as `dry-run-2026-08-12b`.

- **Implemented:** 2026-08-12 (**5 findings recorded as specs; nothing fixed** — report-before-change)
- **Files changed:** five new spec files (listed below), `specs/IMPLEMENTED.md` (this entry). **No plugin file, script or test was modified.**
- **MCP re-check:** **server 1.32.9**, 2026-08-12. Tools called: `get_capabilities`, `get_sample_data(dataset='las-vegas', source='list')`, `download_resource(filename='senzing_entity_specification.md')`, `mapping_workflow(action='start')`, `mapping_workflow(action='advance')`. ⛔ `submit_feedback` **never invoked**, per the skill's absolute rule — one finding drafts an upstream report for maintainer approval and does not send it.
- **Summary:** **Phase 3 conversational walk, maintainer answering as the Bootcamper, started at Module 5** — the module three prior runs named as where to start and never reached. Five turns: module-start apparatus → Steps 1–7 of Phase 1 → the quality gate → Phase 2's verbosity choice → `mapping_workflow` driven from `action='start'` through the first `advance`. **The headline result is that `mapping_workflow` was reached and advanced** — the 8-step stateful workflow whose breakage created this skill, exercised for the first time in four runs, against a real 3,488-record `PPP_LOANS.jsonl` fetched and count-verified per INV-203. Project state for Modules 1–4 was **constructed rather than walked**, so nothing here is evidence about those modules' conversational layer. **Five findings, each written to `specs/` during the walk rather than at the end:** `module5-step2-asks-for-data-module4-already-collected` (Step 2 tells the guide to ask the Bootcamper to place files that the module's own Prerequisites line says are already in `data/raw/`; **confirmed behaviourally** — the maintainer answered a bare "yes", which is indistinguishable between *I placed files* and *they were already there*, so the question cannot detect the state it exists to establish); `module5-quality-score-has-bands-but-no-formula` (Step 6 names three dimensions and defines the presence test exhaustively, then never says how they combine into the single score that routes the gate to the percentage point — measured 100% on spec attributes vs 94.4% across all root keys on the same source, same band here, and the module's own 52%/69%→97% example is where it would cross one); `module5-quality-gate-demands-a-question-its-best-branch-lacks` (the gate orders "Ask exactly one 👉 question to close the turn" and its ≥80% branch — the common one for curated data, and the one this walk landed in — supplies none, so the guide must either fabricate a gate question against INV-056 or disobey the header; the **unsatisfiable-instruction class** phase 3 exists to find); `mapping-workflow-step1-prose-contradicts-its-own-advance-schema` (**upstream** — step 1 states the advance payload twice in incompatible shapes, prose as an object keyed by schema name against a JSON Schema requiring an array with `additionalProperties: false`; resolved **empirically**, the array advanced successfully, and **the plugin was already right** at `:164`/`:206` — draft report in the spec, unsent); and `mapping-workflow-tells-the-guide-not-to-ask-and-the-plugin-never-reconciles-it` (the tool instructs *"Do NOT ask the user to confirm… Do NOT wait for a response. Just advance."* and Module 5 never mentions it — landing immediately after the Bootcamper chose the guided mapping mode that promises to walk them through each decision). **Verified correct on live data, so a later run need not re-derive it:** **INV-198's readiness gate behaved exactly as specified** — 8 of 19 fields resolved (5 spec attributes under a `BUSINESS` usage-type prefix plus 3 structural keys), 11 did not, the fast-path offer was correctly **withheld**, all 11 columns were **named** rather than counted, and routing carried no 👉 because routing is not a choice; **INV-203's collection flow** (stage to `data/temp/`, require HTTP 200 **and** a measured count equal to the catalogue's 3,488, parse the first line, then promote) worked end to end; **INV-138/INV-139's nudge** correctly named only the dial that differed (model already matched, effort undeterminable so the previous stage's row was the sanctioned fallback); **`ground-rules.md:60-65`'s bare-answer carve-out** made a bare "no" acknowledgeable without manufacturing a quote; and the **four rules binding a generated visual deliverable** were all satisfied and *verified rather than assumed* — brand tokens imported from `brand_tokens.py` (not the fallback), zero remote references, field names HTML-escaped, and INV-129 discharged by rasterizing the page headless and **opening the PNG** (19 bars, correct two-colour split, 3.4%–100% widths, numbers drawn). **Coverage limits, stated rather than implied:** the walk stopped at `mapping_workflow` step 2, so **steps 3–8 — field mapping, generation and validation, the test load, and Phase 3 entirely — are unexercised**, and that is where the next run should start; Modules 1–4 were constructed state, so their interaction layer is untested by this run; the hooks do not fire during a walk (phase 2 executes them directly instead); INV-005–INV-009 were exercised across only five turns and **my own compliance is weak evidence** — a clean stretch shows the files *can* produce correct behaviour with a maintainer watching, not that they would without one; Linux only; and no engine was initialized, so nothing here touches loading or resolution. **My own errors, reported:** in phases 1–2 of the same session I duplicated an earlier same-day run (recorded in `dry-run-2026-08-12b`); I mis-invoked `generate_discoveries_pdf.py` with a positional argument instead of `--input` and re-ran it correctly; I captured `$?` after a pipe and read `tail`'s exit status as the script's — the same trap twice in one session, caught both times by re-running without the pipe; and I briefly assumed the hooks lived under `hooks/` when `hooks.json` points at `scripts/`. **One deliberate method choice, disclosed rather than buried:** I presented Step 2 *as written*, knowing from reading that it was wrong, to confirm it behaviourally — the maintainer's answer is the evidence in that finding. **Establishes no invariant.** The run's strongest candidate is named inside `mapping-workflow-tells-the-guide-not-to-ask-and-the-plugin-never-reconciles-it` — *a conversational directive inside an MCP tool response never overrides the bootcamp's interaction invariants* — and is deliberately left for the maintainer's sign-off rather than proposed here, since it would bind every future tool integration.
- **Commit:** `796b297`

## dry-run-2026-08-12b

**Not a spec** — a dated record of a dry-run, following the `dry-run-*` and `production-readiness-audit-*` precedent.

- **Implemented:** 2026-08-12 (**1 finding recorded as a spec; nothing fixed** — report-before-change)
- **Files changed:** `specs/license-request-omits-a-required-field-the-server-demands.md` (new), `specs/IMPLEMENTED.md` (this entry). **No plugin file, script or test was modified.**
- **MCP re-check:** **server 1.32.9**, 2026-08-12. Tools called: `get_capabilities`, `reporting_guide(topic='evaluation')`, plus `submit_feedback`/`search_docs`/`sdk_guide`/`get_sdk_reference`/`mapping_workflow`/`get_sample_data`/`explain_error_code` schemas loaded via `ToolSearch`. ⛔ `submit_feedback` **never invoked** (schema verified only), per the skill's absolute rule.
- **Summary:** Phases 1 and 2 only; phase 3 not run (the maintainer chose "1 + 2, then decide"). ⚠️ **Read this entry's second paragraph before running another dry run — most of this run was wasted.** **One finding, and it is new:** `license-request-omits-a-required-field-the-server-demands`. `module-04-data-collection/SKILL.md:636-637` tells the guide that `license_request` "requires a first name and a work email address …, **optionally** a last name and how they heard about Senzing". The server splits that pair: `lastname` is optional, **`how_heard` is required** — stated in the `submit_feedback` schema's own property description *"(required for license_request)"* and again in the `get_capabilities` manifest. This is the plugin's only step that transmits personal details off the machine, reached only after a pinned consent gate (INV-135), so the sequence is: consent to sending name and work email, then a call built without a field the server requires — and INV-135's requirement that the question state *what is sent* is satisfied against a wrong field list. `tests/test_mcp_call_contracts.py:129-135` already records the correct requirement in its `CONDITIONALLY_REQUIRED` comment, so the repo holds both answers and compares them nowhere; `TestLicenseRequestIsConsentGated` asserts the gate exists, never the payload. **Not runtime-verified and cannot be** — confirming that the server rejects the call would mean sending a real name and work email upstream. **⚠️ My own error, and it is the main lesson of this run: phases 1 and 2 substantially duplicated `dry-run-2026-08-12`, run earlier the same day.** That entry already records "all 42 literals still in enum", "INV-192's gate re-confirmed by live probe", "all seven scripts silent outside a bootcamp", "fold idempotency byte-identical across three runs", "feedback file byte-identical through the normalizer", "recap PDF used the preferred fpdf2 renderer", and INV-110/INV-122/INV-077/INV-127 confirmed. I re-derived every one of those and reported them as verification. I had even seen the `dry-run-2026-08-12` ledger heading in a `citations.py census` earlier in this session and did not connect it. **The `production-readiness-audit` skill opens by requiring its prior entries be read; `dry-run`'s "Before you start" does not, and that asymmetry is what this cost.** The new finding survives because the earlier run's own coverage limit says the `license_request` path was "schema-verified only" — it checked the schema and never compared it against the plugin's prose, which is the one comparison that fails. **Verified correct (re-derived, so cheap to skip next time):** the hook gating claim across all seven scripts; the write-gate's full allow/block matrix including INV-200's resolve-then-compare on a project genuinely at `/tmp/my-bootcamp`; both INV-055 opt-outs (env var **and** the `disable_stop_nudge` preference — the earlier run's entry does not name the preference half); `capture_screenshots.py` rejecting an unknown tab id while listing only INV-155's live six; `session-end.py` staying silent on a recorded-but-absent container, which its own docstring documents as intended; and the `common_confabulations` sweep clean (`close_export` correctly framed, `PHONE_NUMBER` being the correct attribute rather than the confabulation). **Coverage limits:** phase 3 not run, so **INV-005–INV-009 and every gate-ordering rule remain untested** — the gap today's `production-readiness-audit` routed here and which is still open. Linux only, so all `brew`/`scoop`/PowerShell paths are unexercised; `podman` and Apple `container` absent, so INV-195's non-Docker dispatch is still unexercised. `libSz.so` is present on disk but the environment is not initialized, so the viz server's **successful** snapshot path was not reached — only its correct loud failure. **Module 5's `mapping_workflow` was not reached again**, which the earlier run already named as where the next run should start; that is still true and is now two runs deferred. **Establishes no invariant** — a dry run reports.
- **Commit:** `9f49a78`

## the-invariant-to-enforcing-test-link-is-asserted-nowhere

- **Implemented:** 2026-08-12
- **Files changed:** `tests/test_model_guidance_sync.py`, `tests/test_discoveries_pdf.py`, `tests/test_recap_pdf_font_safety.py`, `tests/test_windows_powershell_guidance.py`, `tests/test_generated_html_deliverables.py`, `tests/test_bundled_script_and_production_paths.py`, `tests/test_tab_set_is_singular.py`, `tests/test_partial_row_and_schema_coverage.py`, `tests/test_cord_fetch_integrity.py`, `tests/test_liveness_probe_is_not_a_document_search.py` (docstrings only), `tests/test_invariant_enforcer_citations.py` (new), `.claude/skills/dry-run/coverage_reports.py` (docstring only), `specs/the-invariant-to-enforcing-test-link-is-asserted-nowhere.md` (deviations note), `specs/IMPLEMENTED.md` (this entry)
- **MCP re-check:** **n/a (no Senzing fact).** The spec concerns citations inside `tests/` and `specs/INVARIANTS.md` only. `get_capabilities` reported server **1.32.9**, 2026-08-12, earlier this session; no tool was called for this implementation.
- **Summary:** The invariant→enforcing-test link is now asserted rather than conventional. **Criterion walk, 9 of 9 hold, all runtime-verified.** (1) *All 11 back-citations present* — verified by re-running the extractor, which reports `still missing: NONE`; 10 distinct files, since `test_model_guidance_sync.py` serves both INV-114 and INV-140. (2) *No assertion changed* — `git diff --numstat` shows **41 added lines, 0 deleted** across the ten, and a filtered diff confirms no added line contains `assert`, `def `, `import` or an assignment. (3) *`tests/test_invariant_enforcer_citations.py` asserts existence **and** back-citation* — 4 tests, passing. (4) *Not vacuous* — `EXPECTED_PAIRS = 22` pinned from running the extractor, with a comment recording that it counts **pairs** (one invariant may name several tests and vice versa), plus a named-member check so a count alone cannot pass on the wrong set. (5) *Negative-controlled, both arms, distinct messages* — replacing `INV-188` in its enforcer failed `test_the_named_test_cites_the_invariant_back`; repointing INV-204 at a non-existent file failed `test_the_named_test_file_exists` with its own message. Both mutations verified to land and reverted from `cp` backups. (6) *`coverage_reports.py` records the over-reporting mode* — with INV-183's five rationale-only citations as the worked example, the explicit statement that **a miss on the report is not evidence of coverage**, and a pointer to the new test. (7) ⚠️ *Uncited count* — **79 → 73**, a fall of **6** where the criterion predicted 5; see the deviation. The 6 **masked** invariants did not move, exactly as predicted. (8) *`citations.py verify` clean* at 204; full suite **1756 passed, 3 skipped** (was 1746; +10 across both specs implemented this session). (9) *Stdlib-only* — `re`, `unittest`, `pathlib`; no `plugins/` import. **Deviations recorded:** the sixth invariant to leave the uncited list is **INV-172**, cited by the guard added by `module-03b-hardcodes-the-port-its-own-text-says-may-differ` in the same session — not this spec's doing, recorded so the number is not later read as six gaps closed here; and **a defect in my own guard, caught by its own mutation test** — the first version used `assertIn(ident, path.read_text())`, which prints its container, so the failure message carried an entire ~250-line file and buried the explanation. Changed to `assertTrue(ident in text, msg)` with a comment recording why, and the mutation re-run to confirm the message is now the explanation alone. **Establishes no invariant** — INV-182 and `INVARIANTS.md` rule 3 already govern the discipline; a test is the right enforcement.
- **Commit:** `241eaaf`

## module-03b-hardcodes-the-port-its-own-text-says-may-differ

- **Implemented:** 2026-08-12
- **Files changed:** `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/module-03b-truthset-visualization/phase1-visualization.md`, `tests/test_module_instructions_do_not_hardcode_the_port.py` (new), `specs/module-03b-hardcodes-the-port-its-own-text-says-may-differ.md` (deviations note), `specs/IMPLEMENTED.md` (this entry)
- **MCP re-check:** **n/a (no Senzing fact).** The finding is internal consistency — a file contradicting itself about a port. `get_capabilities` reported server **1.32.9**, 2026-08-12, earlier this session; no tool was called for this implementation.
- **Summary:** Module 03b no longer hardcodes the port at the three sites that act on it. **Criterion walk, 11 of 11 hold, all runtime-verified.** (1) The capture instruction now reads `--url http://localhost:<port>` and states that capturing from a dead port exits 2 and loses every image for the module, permanently. (2) The pinned bootcamper-facing line reads `http://localhost:<port>` with an explicit "never a literal 8080 when 2.3 chose another" note citing INV-172. (3) The recorded checkpoint shows `<port>` in both `"port"` and `"url"`. (4) *The default start command and the collision instruction are unchanged* — a `git diff` filtered to lines mentioning `port 8080`, `--port` or `is in use` returns **nothing**, so `--port 8080`, "records on port 8080", "If port 8080 is in use…" and "Port in use → pass a different `--port`" all stand as written. (5) *`bootcamp_recap.example.md` and `capture_screenshots.py` unchanged* — verified by opening `git status`: neither appears in the changeset, so the sanitized fixture's record of a real run on 8080 (INV-065) and the `--help` usage text (INV-188) are untouched. (6) The new guard passes on the corrected tree, 6 tests. (7) *Not vacuous* — it asserts it scanned >20 instruction files **and** names `phase1-visualization.md` and `phase1-query-visualize.md` by name, since a count alone passes on the wrong files. (8) *Negative-controlled, three mutations, all verified to land and reverted*: restoring the literal at the capture URL failed `check='capture-url'`, at the bootcamper line failed `check='bootcamper-facing-url'`, and in the checkpoint failed `check='recorded-checkpoint'` — while a dedicated test asserts the four legitimate default-port phrasings trip none of the three patterns, which is what stops the obvious "ban 8080" simplification. (9) *`tests/test_snapshot_and_capture_fidelity.py` passes unchanged* at 24 tests — this adds a second, wider guard over module instructions and does not weaken the reference-server one. (10) Full suite **1756 passed, 3 skipped** (was 1746). (11) *Stdlib-only* (`re`, `unittest`, `pathlib`), no `plugins/` import; the rule is stated as behaviour, so it binds a server generated in any language (INV-002/INV-090). **Deviations recorded:** the guard ships a **third** forbidden shape (the recorded checkpoint) beyond the two the criteria named — mutation-verified like the others, disclosed rather than folded in; and the spec's line numbers are pre-fix, since the edits lengthened the file. **Establishes no invariant** — INV-172 already states the rule; this was an unswept site plus a guard narrower than its invariant.
- **Commit:** `241eaaf`

## production-readiness-audit-2026-08-12

**Not a spec** — a dated record of an audit run, following the `deep-dive-audit-*` precedent.

- **Implemented:** 2026-08-12 (findings recorded; **nothing fixed** — report-before-change)
- **Files changed:** `specs/the-invariant-to-enforcing-test-link-is-asserted-nowhere.md` (new), `specs/module-03b-hardcodes-the-port-its-own-text-says-may-differ.md` (new), `specs/IMPLEMENTED.md` (this entry)
- **MCP re-check:** **n/a — both findings are internal consistency and touch no Senzing fact.** `get_capabilities` reported server **1.32.9** earlier this session for unrelated work; no tool was called for either finding.
- **Summary:** Suite green at baseline (**1743 passed, 3 skipped, 1342 subtests**). **Two findings, both written as specs.** (1) **The invariant→enforcing-test link is asserted nowhere.** 22 invariants name a specific test as their enforcer; **11 are not cited back** by the test they name. Because `coverage_reports.py invariants` keys on the ID appearing anywhere under `tests/`, **5 read as unguarded when a dedicated test enforces them** and **6 are masked** by incidental mentions elsewhere — a false all-clear. Four of the eleven were read to confirm the guarantee is real and only the citation is missing; **no phantom tests** (every named file exists). This subsumes `production-readiness-audit-2026-08-11` finding (3), which recorded three of these as "three docstring lines" and **was never actioned** — INV-183 has since been masked, so the report that would have caught it went quiet. (2) **`module-03b/phase1-visualization.md` hardcodes port 8080 at the three sites that act on it** (`:236` capture URL, `:367` the pinned bootcamper-facing line, `:418-419` the report shape) while stating twice in the same file (`:266`, `:408`) that the port may differ. On that branch capture points at a dead URL — losing every screenshot the module produces (INV-122/INV-146) — and the Bootcamper is sent to a port nothing is listening on, which is verbatim INV-172's originating failure. Module 07 already does it correctly with `<port>` at both parallel sites. **Root cause is a guard narrower than its invariant:** `test_snapshot_and_capture_fidelity.py:76-81` enforces INV-172 against `senzing_viz_server.py`'s snapshot path only and scans no module instructions, so the third site was never swept. **Method note — this closes a gap both prior runs disclosed:** drifted repetition had been measured but never audited. `conformance.py duplication` finds only *verbatim* passages, which is structurally blind to drift, so a near-duplicate scan (0.82–0.995 similarity, cross-file, filtered to pairs whose numbers or invariant IDs differ) was built for this run; finding (2) came from it at 0.894. **Verified correct and not re-checked next run:** `module-07`'s `phase2-discover.md` / `phase2b-discover.md` are both legitimate Phase 2a/2b files, referenced from `SKILL.md:74-75` and by three tests — not a stale duplicate; every test file named by an invariant exists (no phantom-test defect); the top verbatim-duplication pairs are per-step apparatus restatement, which is INV-183 and not redundancy; `citations.py verify` clean at 204 invariants. **Coverage limits, stated plainly:** the conversational invariants **INV-005–INV-009 and every gate-ordering rule were NOT tested and cannot be** by reading — they need `dry-run` phase 3. The 14 `conformance.py rules` hits were **not exhaustively read** this run; the two prior runs triaged them and one resolved to INV-199/INV-200, and I did not repeat that pass. The forward sweep was **not** re-run across all 204 invariants — it was scoped to the 31 enumerating invariants plus the two classes above, on the strength of having read `INVARIANTS.md` in full earlier the same session. `podman` and Apple `container` remain absent, so INV-195's non-Docker dispatch is still unexercised; no Windows host, so INV-167/INV-199's Windows halves remain text-asserted only. **Establishes no new invariant** — neither finding proposes one: (1) is enforcement of existing discipline and (2) is an unswept site of INV-172, which already states the rule.

## cord-download-rate-limit-is-saved-as-data

- **Implemented:** 2026-08-12
- **Files changed:** `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/module-04-data-collection/SKILL.md`, `tests/test_cord_fetch_integrity.py` (new), `specs/INVARIANTS.md` (INV-203 + its index entry), `specs/cord-download-rate-limit-is-saved-as-data.md` (invariants-introduced + deviations notes), `specs/IMPLEMENTED.md` (this entry)
- **MCP re-check:** **server 1.32.9**, 2026-08-12 — **changed (two of the spec's facts).** Tools called: `get_capabilities` (version), `get_sample_data(dataset='las-vegas', source='list')` (per-source `record_count`), `get_sample_data(dataset='las-vegas', source='GLEIF'/'EQUIFAX', limit=1)` (the `download_url` / `source_download_url` / `download_url_max_records` citation fields), plus live HTTP fetches of the returned `download_url`. **The condition still reproduces** — fetching four `las-vegas` sources back to back, `OPEN-OWNERSHIP` and `US-LABOR-VIOLATIONS` returned 43-byte prose bodies. **Changed (1):** that response carries a real **HTTP 429**, refuting the spec's *"no HTTP error for a caller to branch on"* and its root cause's *"200-ish body … rather than a machine-readable error"*; measured `http=429` on both. The status check therefore **leads** the implemented fix, and the spec's upstream-report premise is refuted — nothing was sent. **Changed (2):** the citation carries `download_url_max_records: 10000` and the cap bites (`NOMINO-RISK`, MCP `record_count` 14,119 → **exactly 10,000** records fetched), so the spec's bare-equality "mismatch = failed collection" would fail **6 of the 11** `las-vegas` sources on correct data; expected count is `min(record_count, cap)` for a `download_url` fetch and exactly `record_count` for `source_download_url`.
- **Summary:** Module 4 gains one anchored canonical block, `#cord-fetch-integrity`, referenced (not restated) from the generated-scenario fetch path, the registry schema and Data File Validation. **Criterion walk, 7 of 7.** (1) *Count comparison, mismatch = failed collection, not a warning* — check 2 of the block takes the expected figure from `get_sample_data(…, source='list')` and states "a **failed collection, not a warning**", capped per the MCP re-check above; asserted by `TheCountComparisonIsPrescribed` (5 tests). (2) *Throttling named, with retry/backoff and spaced fetches* — check 1 quotes the 43-byte body verbatim, names HTTP 429 as the decisive test, calls out that `curl -sS -o <file> <url>` exits **0** and writes the prose body anyway, and instructs a one-second backoff plus a pause between sequential fetches; asserted by `TheRateLimitCaseIsNamedAndHandled` (6 tests). The backoff was **verified live rather than only prescribed**: the same four-source fetch that lost two sources returned all four complete at full counts when each request retried. (3) *Nothing unverified reaches the final name; staging inside the project* — check 3 stages at `data/temp/<source>.jsonl` (the scratch directory INV-050's tree already provides) and promotes to `data/raw/<source>.jsonl` only once checks 1–2 pass, project-relative, system temp explicitly refused (INV-200); asserted by `AnUnverifiedFetchNeverReachesItsFinalName` (3 tests). (4) *Registry records both counts* — the Data Source Registry schema gains `expected_record_count` and redefines `record_count` as the count you **measured** (it was "if known, else null" — the optionality the spec named as half the defect), and both checks are named for `validation_checks` (`http_status_ok`, `record_count_matches_expected`), which Step 7 reads back; asserted by `BothCountsAreRecorded` (3 tests). (5) *Negative-controlled guard* — `tests/test_cord_fetch_integrity.py`, **29 tests**; **four mutations each verified to land** (grep count of the original string → 0) and each failing the suite: dropped retry-with-backoff, dropped the `min()` cap rule, downgraded mismatch to "possible concern", dropped the 2xx check. The file was restored byte-identical afterwards (`cmp`). (6) *Re-verify live at implementation time* — done; still reproduces, with the two deviations above recorded in the spec. (7) *Cross-platform and language-agnostic, not a `wc -l` pipeline* — the block asks for "a count, in whatever language the Bootcamper chose" and gives the status route for an HTTP client, `curl --fail`, and PowerShell's `Invoke-WebRequest`; `TheCheckStaysLanguageAgnostic` asserts `wc -l`, `wc -c` and `grep -c` appear nowhere in it. **One correction outside the spec's scope but required by criterion 1:** `SKILL.md:183` told the guide to present `download_url` *"so the bootcamper can download the full JSONL file"* — untrue for the 6 sources above the cap and irreconcilable with the new count rule; replaced by the two-URL distinction (capped slice needing only `mcp.senzing.com`, versus the complete file needing `senzing.com` egress). **Not runtime-verified:** no macOS or Windows host was available, so criterion 7's cross-platform half rests on the instruction being platform-neutral (test-asserted) rather than on execution; the throttling condition itself is network-dependent and unreachable by the offline suite, exactly as the spec says. Full suite **1644 passed, 3 skipped**. **Deviations** recorded in the spec's `## Deviations from this spec, and why (2026-08-12)`: the HTTP-429 correction and its effect on both the fix's ordering and the upstream question; the `download_url_max_records` cap and the resulting `min()` rule; the "full JSONL file" correction; `data/temp/` chosen over inventing a directory (INV-202); and the backoff verified live. **Establishes `INV-203`** — maintainer-approved 2026-08-12, appended to `specs/INVARIANTS.md` beneath the marker and indexed under *Data quality, mapping and validation gates*, with the bidirectional `## Invariants introduced` note in the spec. Its wording is **wider than the spec proposed**: the spec offered only "record count matches the count the MCP server reported for it", and the approved statement adds the **2xx** clause and the `min(record_count, download_url_max_records)` cap clause, both of which came from this session's re-check rather than from the spec's text — the bare rule as offered would have been unsatisfiable on 6 of the 11 `las-vegas` sources.
- **Commit:** `53d4ee4`

## dry-run-2026-08-12

**Not a spec** — a dated record of a dry-run, following the `production-readiness-audit-*` and `deep-dive-audit-*` precedent. **FINAL.** First written mid-run (so the findings would survive a lost session), then completed when the maintainer stopped the walk at the end of Data collection. The "Final update" paragraph at the end of the Summary records everything found after the interim was written; the rest of the entry stands as first written.

- **Implemented:** 2026-08-12 (**10 findings recorded as specs; nothing fixed** — report-before-change)
- **Files changed:** `tests/test_mcp_call_contracts.py` (`CONTRACT_VERIFIED_ON` refreshed to 2026-08-12 after re-verifying the whole contract), `specs/IMPLEMENTED.md` (this entry), plus 8 new spec files listed below. **No plugin file was modified.**
- **MCP re-check:** **server 1.32.9** throughout (`get_capabilities` → `server_info.server_version`; docs index `2026-08-11 20:52 UTC`). Tools called: `get_capabilities`, `search_docs`, `sdk_guide`, `generate_scaffold`, `get_sdk_reference`, `get_sample_data`, `explain_error_code`, `reporting_guide`. ⛔ `submit_feedback` **never invoked during the run** (schema verified only), per the skill's absolute rule. **After the run closed**, the maintainer approved the exact text of one upstream report and it was sent as `category='bug'` on 2026-08-12 — the eval-licence duration contradiction (finding 6). That send is a separate maintainer-authorised action, not part of the dry run; anonymous, so no reply is possible. Outcomes: **all 42** action/topic/category/workflow/platform/dataset/language literals still in enum; required-parameter lists unchanged; `tool_count` 13 matches `MCP_TOOLS`; **one fact fixed upstream** (`explain_error_code('SENZ7426')`); **one server self-contradiction** (eval-licence duration); **one dated citation gone stale** (`generate_scaffold(workflow='initialize')` snippet inventory).
- **Summary:** All three phases run. **Phase 1 (MCP contracts):** clean on the class that originally broke Module 5 — every literal in enum, the five `common_confabulations` all absent (and `register_data_source` used correctly), and the plugin touches neither `SZ_ENTITY_INCLUDE_RECORD_FEATURES` nor `SZ_ENTITY_INCLUDE_NAME_ONLY_RELATIONS`, so the v4.1.0 `FEATURES`→`FEATURE_IDS` rename and the v4 name-only reclassification do not reach it. INV-192's gate and INV-132's correction both re-confirmed by live probe. **Phase 2 (hooks/scripts):** all seven scripts silent outside a bootcamp; **the write-gate boundary fix (`8a94cac`) confirmed live** — the `config/../../escape.txt` → `$HOME` case that was ALLOWED on 2026-08-11 now blocks with the boundary message, 16/16 probes as expected; fold idempotency byte-identical across three runs; feedback file byte-identical through the normalizer; recap PDF used the **preferred fpdf2** renderer, not the stdlib fallback; INV-110 (junk → no PDF, exit 1), INV-122 (absent tab skipped, manifest records it), INV-077 (no snapshot without a loadable engine) and INV-127 (14 sources → 14 distinct encodings, order-independent) all confirmed. **Phase 3 (conversational walk, maintainer answering):** 28 turns covering onboarding preface → Bootcamp preparation → Entity Resolution Concepts (primer + 4-item knowledge check + recap write) → Discover the Business Problem (full apparatus, Phase 1 steps 1–4b, Phase 2 steps 9–17) → SDK setup (Steps 1–9), **extended two modules past the documented scope at the maintainer's request**, ending with a **real Senzing engine running** against a project-local SQLite datastore (`SzDiagnostic.check_repository_performance` succeeded — the engine-class call Step 9 requires, not a version query). **8 findings, each written to `specs/` before any further work:** `relay-the-default-flags-production-caution` (the server's DEFAULT_FLAGS production caution is relayed nowhere, while graduation copies that code into `production/src/`); `explain-error-code-now-owns-senz7426` (**highest severity** — the plugin tells the guide *not to relay* `explain_error_code('SENZ7426')`, which now names SUPPORTPATH first; a workaround for a gap fixed upstream, and a test whitelists the stale claim as "safety text"); `scaffold-banner-ignores-fresh-and-seeded-modes`; `preparation-recap-template-contradicts-its-own-rules`; `preparation-summarises-the-model-nudge-trigger-as-the-forbidden-comparison` (behavioural — suppresses the switch question for the first four stages of every Core run); `step14-value-proposition-query-is-bm25-hostile-with-no-fallback`; `mcp-tools-disagree-on-eval-license-duration` (**upstream, not yet sent** — needs maintainer approval); `refresh-the-initialize-workflow-snippet-count-in-step4`. **Verified correct and not re-checkable more cheaply later:** the `ground-rules.md:60-65` bare-"no" acknowledgment carve-out (the originating run's first finding, now fixed); INV-198's correction that CORD is *not* uniformly pre-mapped (`PPP_LOANS` returns spec attributes plus eleven raw columns — confirmed live); the plugin's EULA relay, which is **more** thorough than the server's (per-platform variable table, macOS lowercase trap); INV-200's argument-level half (the MCP-suggested `/tmp/sqlite/G2C.db` overridden to `database/G2C.db`); Step 8a's seeding precondition (`get_default_config_id` genuinely raised on the fresh datastore); and `tests/test_model_guidance_sync.py` keeping the authoritative and derived model tables aligned. **Coverage limits, stated rather than implied:** the suite and every probe ran on **Linux only**, so the write-gate boundary fix, the INV-050 tree guard and all `brew`/`scoop` paths remain unexercised on macOS and Windows; Step 1b's *execute* branch could not run (`sudo` needs a password) so only its check-and-offer logic is verified; the hooks do not fire during a walk, so the `Stop` closing-question net was tested only by direct execution in phase 2; `MCP_TOOLS` is still a dated copy, so a 14th server tool stays invisible; and my own compliance is weak evidence — a clean stretch shows the files *can* produce correct behaviour with a maintainer watching. **My own errors, reported:** I corrupted 7 `SKILL.md` files with a basename-keyed backup in a mutation harness (caught via `git status`, restored from HEAD, harness fixed, re-run — the first run's verdicts were void); I twice reported an inconclusive measurement as if it were a result (`\n## ` section count, before/after-only fold check) and re-measured both times; I leaked internal turn mechanics into bootcamper text once; I over-promised "database configuration" while three steps remained, then ran them rather than skipping; I cited the *derived* model table for two turns before noticing `ground-rules.md` is authoritative (they agreed); and I built a version-mismatch hypothesis on a four-turn-old reading that dissolved on re-check. **Establishes no invariant** — a dry run reports; registering rules is `implement-spec`'s job after sign-off. **Final update (end of run).** The walk continued past the interim through **System verification**, **Truth Set visualization** and **Data collection**, ending at the start of Data Quality, Mapping, and Transformation — **six modules complete**, five past phase 3's documented scope. Two further findings, bringing the total to **10**: `module3-connectivity-probe-still-uses-the-deprecated-search-docs-call` (Module 3 Step 1 probes liveness with `search_docs`, which `onboarding-flow.md:46-48` already ⛔-deprecated for exactly that use — the **third** cross-module non-propagation of this run) and ⚠️ `cord-download-rate-limit-is-saved-as-data` (**the most operationally significant finding**: fetching four CORD sources back to back, two returned `Rate limit exceeded. Try again in 1 second.` **as the response body** — HTTP success, non-empty file, one line of English prose written into `data/raw/<source>.jsonl`. Caught only by comparing line counts against the counts `get_sample_data` had already reported. The plugin has **no** rate-limit awareness anywhere, and `SKILL.md:296` asks for a *"plausible record count"* — a judgement, not a comparison. The proposed remedy was **implemented and validated in the walk**: stage each fetch, require both an exact count match and a JSON-parsable first line, promote only then, back off and retry otherwise — after which all four matched exactly at 9,033 records). **What the extension bought that the documented scope could not:** a **real engine** initialized against a project-local SQLite datastore, seeded default config, and an engine-class call (`SzDiagnostic.check_repository_performance`) rather than a version query; **real entity resolution twice over** — 4 synthetic records → 2 entities exactly as predicted by construction, and the 159-record Truth Set → **84 entities** (55 merged, 17 cross-source, 71 relationships) rendered in the live web app with all ten documented endpoints returning 200 and `/api/stats` matching the load field-for-field; the **model/effort nudge exercised in all four branches** (model-only, effort-only, both, neither) from one continuous session; the tab-coverage check **passing against a real external denominator** (6 of 6) for the first time in the run; and the earlier `SENZ7426` failure explained end-to-end — the same call that failed with `{"PIPELINE": {}}` succeeded once `SUPPORTPATH` was set, which is a live demonstration of `explain-error-code-now-owns-senz7426`. **Four near-findings were checked and dissolved rather than filed** (CORD-pre-mapped vs the mapping-complexity invariant — already corrected by INV-198 the day before; Module 3's "Opt-Out Gate" — a trigger-phrase handler, not a question; `/api/why`'s envelope — conforms exactly to `visualization-api-reference.md:399-405`; and a dpkg-vs-SDK version mismatch built on a four-turn-old reading that the host had changed underneath). **Four further errors of my own, all caught by inspecting artifacts rather than exit codes:** omitted `--title "Senzing Truth Set"` so the snapshot carried the default header (found by *reading the PNG*); captured screenshots from `--html` when the live `--url` is prescribed; omitted `--query` so Search / Probe rendered an empty result panel; and `pkill -f 'senzing_viz_server.py'` matched my own command line and killed the shell. Errors 1–3 share a shape worth stating: the module specified the argument and I skipped it — the plugin was right every time. **Coverage limits added by the extension:** the apt **upgrade** path could not run (`sudo` needs a password), so Step 1b's execute branch is unexercised; `submit_feedback` was never invoked, so the `license_request` path is schema-verified only; and Module 5's `mapping_workflow` — the 8-step stateful workflow this skill was created for — was **not reached**, which is where a next run should start.

## inv050-tree-has-no-reachability-guard

- **Implemented:** 2026-08-11
- **Files changed:** `tests/test_invariant_layout_tree.py` (new), `specs/INVARIANTS.md` (INV-202 + index entry under **The development record itself**), `specs/inv050-tree-has-no-reachability-guard.md` (invariant note)
- **MCP re-check:** **n/a — no Senzing fact.** The spec states this itself; the subject is internal consistency between INV-050's tree and what ships. No tool was called for it. (`get_capabilities` was called earlier in the same session for unrelated specs and reported server **1.32.9**.)
- **Summary:** **All eight criteria hold; seven are runtime-verified and one is partly not.** (1) `tests/test_invariant_layout_tree.py::TreeIsFullyAccountedFor::test_every_entry_is_referenced_or_annotated` walks the tree and passes — `extract_tree()` uses `line.partition("#")` and carries the comment on every `Entry`, so the column is intact. (2) **Each of the three parsing hazards has its own assertion:** the comment column is pinned on the *predicate* rather than the extractor (`test_the_predicate_requires_the_comment_column` feeds a synthetic unreferenced entry with and without `(reserved)`, so it stays valid whatever happens to any real entry); the `backups/` continuation line is counted, not merely skipped (`test_the_continuation_line_is_not_an_entry`); and the placeholder resolves on its prefix (`test_a_placeholder_resolves_on_its_prefix`, plus `test_the_placeholder_entry_is_still_a_placeholder` guarding that the tree still *has* a placeholder, or that test would be theatre). (3) **Not vacuous:** counts pinned at 24 files / 30 dirs / 1 continuation line, **derived by running the extractor today, not copied** — the superseded spec says 53 entries / 23 files and disagrees — with a comment recording exactly what they count (root excluded, continuation lines excluded, placeholder counted as one file); a corpus-size assertion covers the other half, since an empty corpus would make every entry look unreferenced. (4) **Negative-controlled, both mutations verified to land by reading the file back:** a fictional **unannotated** entry fails the guard, the **same entry annotated `(reserved)`** passes it (only the count assertion objects, correctly — the tree genuinely gained an entry), and both were reverted with `git diff specs/INVARIANTS.md` confirming byte-identical. (5) **Checked by opening the file, not from memory:** `session_log.jsonl`, `visualization_tracker.json` and `completion_summary.md` appear in `tests/test_invariant_layout_tree.py` **only in the module docstring**, explaining that they are correctly accounted for — no assertion references them, so the false claim is not re-enshrined. (6) INV-050 itself is untouched (`git diff` empty). (7) Stdlib-only: `re`, `unittest`, `pathlib`; no `plugins/` import (INV-108). (8) **Partly not runtime-verified:** the code has no platform branch — nothing shells out to `grep`, traversal is `pathlib.rglob`, and directory probes match file *content* rather than filesystem paths — but the suite ran on **Linux only**, so macOS and Windows are asserted by construction, not exercised; that needs a `dry-run` on each host. **One finding beyond the spec:** entry names are **not unique** — `data/backups/` (annotated `(reserved)`) and the top-level `backups/` (a real produced directory) both reduce to `backups/`, so a name-keyed dict would silently drop one of two entries that differ in exactly the way that matters. `test_entry_names_are_not_unique` pins it. Suite **1612 passed, 3 skipped, 1271 subtests** (was 1602/3/1270; +10 tests, +1 subtest). **Establishes INV-202** (maintainer-approved wording). No `## Deviations` note was needed — nothing in the spec was contradicted.
- **Commit:** `fdd9b8a`

## required-params-guard-covers-two-of-nine-tools

- **Files changed:** `tests/test_mcp_call_contracts.py`, `specs/INVARIANTS.md` (INV-201 + index entry under **MCP sourcing and tool contracts**), `specs/required-params-guard-covers-two-of-nine-tools.md` (deviation + invariant notes)
- **MCP re-check:** **changed — server is 1.32.9, not the spec's 1.32.8 (2026-08-11).** Every required-parameter list in the spec's table re-read this session from the live tool schemas and confirmed unchanged, so the seven additions landed as tabulated. One row of the spec is wrong about *provenance* and the implementation follows the server: `mapping_workflow`'s `required` array is **empty** — its `file_paths` + `workspace_dir` requirement is contract-level prose, conditional on `action='start'`, not schema-required as the spec's column header says. Tools called: `get_capabilities` (version, tool_count 13) plus the loaded `inputSchema` of all 13 tools.
- **Summary:** **All seven criteria hold and every one is runtime-verified.** (1) `REQUIRED_PARAMS` now carries **9** entries (was 2), each with its provenance marked in a trailing comment — schema-required vs contract-required — so the dict no longer implies all nine come from the schema. (2) Completeness is a **partition**, not a list: `MCP_TOOLS` (13, server 1.32.9) must be exactly covered by `REQUIRED_PARAMS` ∪ `CONDITIONALLY_REQUIRED` ∪ `NO_REQUIRED_PARAMS`, with `test_the_three_sets_partition_every_server_tool`, `test_no_tool_is_classified_twice` and `test_every_referenced_tool_is_classified`; the reference scan is name-based, not `tool(`-based, because the plugin calls `submit_feedback` and `get_capabilities` by name in prose and a paren-only scan reported them uncalled. (3) `find_examples` is exempted **with its reason in the code** (requires one of `query` | `repo`+`file_path` | `repo`+`list_files`). (4) Re-verification clause discharged — see MCP re-check. (5) **Negative-controlled per tool: all 11 tool+param mutations caught, each verified landed** by re-running the scan after redaction; because the guard is `any()`-across-callers the harness had to strip the token from *every* calling file (14 for `search_docs`, 9 each for `sdk_guide`/`explain_error_code`). Two completeness mutations also caught — an unclassified 14th tool added to `MCP_TOOLS` (1 failure), and `generate_scaffold` dropped from `REQUIRED_PARAMS`, reproducing the original defect shape (2 failures). (6) The action-enum and `FORBIDDEN_WORKSPACE_DIRS` checks are **untouched** and pass. (7) Stdlib-only — `re`, `unittest`, `pathlib`; no `plugins/` import (INV-108). **One defect the spec did not predict, found while implementing and fixed here:** the existing matcher was `param in text`, and `error_code` is a substring of `explain_error_code`, so that tool's entry **could never fail** — adding the spec's seven entries under the old matcher would have shipped a guard that reads as coverage and cannot fail. Matching is now token-boundary (`mentions()`), pinned by `test_the_param_scan_is_not_satisfied_by_the_tool_name` and negative-controlled by reverting it to the substring form (1 failure). **No plugin change was required**, as the spec predicted: every one of the 11 mutations failed only after redaction, so all call sites are correct today. `CONTRACT_VERIFIED_ON` moved to 2026-08-11 and `MCP_SERVER_VERSION` added. Suite **1602 passed, 3 skipped, 1269 subtests** (was 1596/3/1263). **Deviations recorded** in the spec's `## Deviations from this spec, and why (2026-08-11)`: the 1.32.9 version bump; `mapping_workflow`'s contract-vs-schema provenance; `submit_feedback` classified **conditional** rather than the spec's "genuinely none", because its requirements are conditional on `category`; the unpredicted substring-vacuity defect; and the deliberate retention of `any()` semantics. **Establishes INV-201** (maintainer-approved wording).

## inv200-overstates-what-the-write-gate-blocks

- **Implemented:** 2026-08-11
- **Files changed:** `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/scripts/write-gate.py`, `tests/test_write_gate.py`, `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/hooks/README.md`, `specs/inv200-overstates-what-the-write-gate-blocks.md` (deviation note)
- **MCP re-check:** **n/a — no Senzing fact.** The spec states this itself; the finding is internal behaviour only. No tool was called for it. (`get_capabilities` was called this session for the companion spec, and reported server 1.32.9.)
- **Summary:** **Maintainer chose reading (b) — the gate was fixed, not the invariant.** `write-gate.py:149` now blocks the fall-through, so any resolved target outside the project is blocked and INV-200 is true as written, **including its `..` clause**: `norm()` resolves the escape and the boundary check blocks wherever it lands. **Two files the spec predicted were deliberately not changed, because (b) is the reading that makes them already correct:** `specs/INVARIANTS.md` (INV-200 is left exactly as written — the spec proposed editing it only under reading (a), and under (b) the code now satisfies it, ⛔ so it was neither corrected, renumbered nor deleted) and `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/bootcamp-onboarding/ground-rules.md` (`:224`'s "ALL files stay inside the working directory" and "the write-gate enforces the write half" are now both true of the hook). Criteria: (1) gate blocks the fall-through ✅; (2) `test_dotdot_escape_blocked` reworked — it previously built `config/../../..` + `/tmp/evil.txt`, so the escape landed in `/tmp` and was blocked **as temp**, its own comment saying so; it now escapes to `/opt/not-the-project/x.txt`, which no prefix, substring or temp env var names ✅; (3) the fall-through has its own test, `test_out_of_project_target_naming_no_list_is_blocked`, over three probes (`$HOME`, `/opt`, `/etc`), non-vacuous because the boundary message can only come from the fall-through ✅; (4) **n/a — an (a)-only criterion**; (5) the out-of-project write set was **enumerated before the change and is empty** — no file under `plugins/` names a write target outside the project (the feedback file is `docs/feedback/...`, the env script is project-local per INV-166/167/175), the hook is `PreToolUse` on **Write/Edit only** (`hooks/hooks.json:37`) so `Bash` SDK installs to `/opt/senzing` are unaffected, it activates only when the cwd *is* the project, and INV-199 already forbids out-of-project writes categorically; no exemption list was needed ✅; (6) **negative-controlled, mutation verified landed**: removing `block(OUTSIDE_MSG)` fails 6 tests ✅; (7) **Windows is not runtime-verified — no Windows host**, and macOS is not either; the logic is string-only (`norm()` never touches the filesystem) and the `%TEMP%`/`%TMP%` and `/appdata/local/temp/` paths are exercised on Linux, but neither platform was itself exercised — that needs a `dry-run` on each. **The substantive deviation is what (b) cost the tests.** Once every out-of-project path blocks, the exit code can no longer say *which* branch blocked, and the env-var branch — added nine hours earlier under `write-gate-location-logic-is-unregistered` precisely because no static list can match a relocated temp dir — becomes untestable by outcome. A second message (`OUTSIDE_MSG`) restores the distinction and three cases now assert the message: `test_relocated_temp_dir_blocked_via_env_only`, `test_the_relocated_temp_probe_is_not_caught_by_a_static_list` (asserted ALLOW before; cannot any more), and `test_home_personal_tmp_allowed` → renamed `test_home_personal_tmp_is_not_mistaken_for_system_temp` (also asserted ALLOW before; `~/tmp` is outside the project so it now blocks, and the message is what still proves it is not classified as *system* temp). **Proved necessary by mutation:** deleting the whole `TMPDIR`/`TEMP`/`TMP` loop leaves the suite green under exit-code-only assertions and fails 3 tests with the message assertions. Also recorded: the temp lists are **no longer load-bearing for the block decision** — they only select the more specific message. Suite **1602 passed, 3 skipped, 1269 subtests** (was 1596/3/1263). **Deviations recorded** in the spec's `## Deviations from this spec, and why (2026-08-11)`: reading (b) and what it made moot; the empty out-of-project set with its evidence; the three exit-code→message conversions and the renamed test; the temp lists becoming message-only; `hooks/README.md` changed though the spec did not list it (two places described the gate as temp/Downloads-only); and the un-run platforms. **Establishes no new invariant** — it makes INV-200 true rather than adding a rule. A candidate rule ("a test pinning one gate branch must assert the message, not the exit code") was drafted and the maintainer declined to register it; it lives in the test file's docstring with a landed negative control.
- **Commit:** `8a94cac`

## write-gate-location-logic-is-unregistered

- **Implemented:** 2026-08-11
- **Files changed:** `specs/INVARIANTS.md` (INV-200 + index entry), `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/bootcamp-onboarding/ground-rules.md`, `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/scripts/write-gate.py` (docstring), `tests/test_write_gate.py`, `tests/test_mcp_call_contracts.py`, `specs/write-gate-location-logic-is-unregistered.md` (deviation + invariant notes)
- **MCP re-check:** **confirmed, server 1.32.8, 2026-08-11.** The rule asserts no Senzing fact; the one Senzing detail written into shipped text — that `mapping_workflow` and `analyze_record` require `workspace_dir` and that the server's contract warns *"do NOT assume /tmp exists"* — was re-confirmed this session via `get_capabilities`, not carried from the spec. Tool called: `get_capabilities`.
- **Summary:** **All seven criteria hold, and the negative control found a real gap in the invariant itself.** (1) **INV-200 recorded** at the next unused ID with maintainer-approved wording and an index entry under **Platform, shell, encoding and file placement** in the same edit. (2) `ground-rules.md:224` and `write-gate.py`'s module docstring both cite it; the docstring now names the hook's two independent checks and which invariant governs each. (3) `tests/test_write_gate.py` cites it — **and `tests/test_mcp_call_contracts.py:50` too**, a second enforcing file the spec missed. (4) **INV-109 unchanged, verified by opening it.** (5) **`write-gate.py`'s INV-001 citation left alone, verified by reading it in context** — it explains consulting all three temp env vars *"so no platform is covered less well than the others"*, which is INV-001's subject, not this rule's. (6) Behaviour unchanged — but see the deviation: two tests were **added**, against the spec's "add no new test". (7) Windows enforcement is **not runtime-verified** — no Windows host — so the new test asserts the env-var branch blocks a relocated temp dir by *name*, never that a real `%TEMP%` on Windows is blocked; that needs a Windows `dry-run`. Suite **1596 passed, 3 skipped, 1263 subtests** (was 1594; +2). **Three mutations, all caught**: the location check disabled (6 failures), the env-var loop removed (3), only `TMPDIR` consulted (2). **Deviations recorded:** the rule binds **six** shipped sites and the spec named two — the incomplete-application class in the audit's own spec for the third time in one day; that sweep widened INV-200 to bind **MCP tool arguments** (`workspace_dir`), which the spec's draft omitted entirely; and most importantly ⚠️ **the first negative control showed INV-200 claiming enforcement that did not exist.** Deleting `write-gate.py`'s whole `for var in ("TMPDIR", "TEMP", "TMP")` loop left the suite green, because `test_tmpdir_env_blocked` uses `tempfile.mkdtemp()` (returns `/tmp/...` on Linux, caught by the static prefix first) and `test_windows_temp_env_blocked` passes the literal string `%TEMP%` (caught by the substring list) — two tests named for a branch neither reaches. That branch exists for macOS `/var/folders/...` and Windows `AppData\Local\Temp`, which no static prefix can enumerate. `test_relocated_temp_dir_blocked_via_env_only` now targets a path no static list matches, with `test_the_relocated_temp_probe_is_not_caught_by_a_static_list` guarding that the probe stays outside those lists so the new test cannot start passing for the wrong reason. **Establishes INV-200** (maintainer-approved wording).
- **Commit:** `32a5832`

## production-readiness-audit-2026-08-11b

**Not a spec** — a dated record of an audit run, following the `deep-dive-audit-*` precedent.

- **Implemented:** 2026-08-11 (finding recorded; **nothing fixed** — report-before-change)
- **Files changed:** `specs/write-gate-location-logic-is-unregistered.md` (new), `specs/IMPLEMENTED.md` (this entry)
- **MCP re-check:** **n/a — the finding is internal consistency and touches no Senzing fact.** No tool was called this run.
- **Summary:** Second run the same day, deliberately **scoped** rather than repeated: the delta since the first run (commit `91cdb54`) plus the gaps the first run disclosed. Suite green (**1594 passed, 3 skipped, 1259 subtests**). **One finding.** The write-gate's **location logic** — "ALL files stay inside the working directory. Never `/tmp`, `%TEMP%`, or `~/Downloads`" — is the plugin's base file-placement guarantee, enforced by a `PreToolUse` hook whose own comment states the intent (`write-gate.py:97`) and by **12 dedicated tests** in `tests/test_write_gate.py`, and it is registered in **no invariant**. INV-109 governs the same hook's *secret* check and its wording names the gap itself: *"the secret check runs independently of **the location logic**"*. Recorded in `specs/write-gate-location-logic-is-unregistered.md` with a drafted invariant awaiting maintainer sign-off. **Two probes were checked rather than assumed, and both dissolved**: `write-gate.py`'s lone `INV-001` citation looked like the wrong-citation class until read in context — it sits at `:117` explaining why all three of `TMPDIR`/`TEMP`/`TMP` are consulted *"so no platform is covered less well than the others"*, which is exactly INV-001's subject, so it is correct and was left alone; and a broad vocabulary sweep of `INVARIANTS.md` (`inside the working/project`, `project-relative`, `outside the project`, `write-gate`, `scratch`, `/tmp`, `%TEMP%`, `Downloads`) confirmed no invariant covers the rule under different words before the finding was written. **My own miss, reported plainly:** the first run of this audit did not find this, and INV-199's citation was added to the second half of the very `ground-rules.md` bullet whose first half states this rule uncited — I edited that bullet hours earlier and did not notice. `conformance.py rules` structurally cannot surface it now: it reports hard rules whose **section** cites no invariant, and adding INV-199 to that section made it read covered while its broader claim is not. **Coverage limits:** the conversational invariants INV-005-INV-009 and every gate-ordering rule were **not tested** and need `dry-run` phase 3. This run did **not** re-sweep all 199 invariants, did not audit concision, and did not exhaustively read the 15 hard-rule hits — the first run's disclosures on those still stand unchanged. `podman` and Apple `container` remain absent, so INV-195's non-Docker dispatch is still unexercised; INV-199's Windows half is asserted as text, never as behaviour. **Establishes no new invariant** — the run proposes one inside its spec for sign-off; registering it is `implement-spec`'s job after approval, never the audit's.
- **Commit:** `32a5832`

## production-readiness-audit-2026-08-11

**Not a spec** — a dated record of an audit run, following the `deep-dive-audit-*` precedent.

⛔ **Correction, 2026-08-11 (same day): finding (1) was WRONG and is withdrawn.** `config/session_log.jsonl`, `config/visualization_tracker.json` and `docs/completion_summary.md` are each annotated `(reserved)` in the INV-050 tree — annotated deliberately on 2026-07-17 by `layout-tree-reconciliation` (commit `cc46a55`), which had already found and settled this exact question. The audit's extraction stripped the comment column (`line.split("#")[0]`) before matching, discarding the only place the annotation lives, then treated its own scan as a verdict. Re-run keeping comments: **zero tree entries are unaccounted for.** `specs/inv050-layout-tree-names-three-artifacts-nothing-produces.md` carries the full correction and MUST NOT be implemented. Findings (2) and (3) were verified by reading and stand.

- **Implemented:** 2026-08-11 (findings recorded; **nothing fixed** — report-before-change)
- **Files changed:** `specs/inv050-layout-tree-names-three-artifacts-nothing-produces.md` (new), `specs/never-modify-global-shell-config-is-unregistered.md` (new), `specs/IMPLEMENTED.md` (this entry)
- **MCP re-check:** **n/a — every finding is internal consistency and touches no Senzing fact.** One `sdk_guide(topic='install', platform='macos_arm', language='java')` call was made on server 1.32.8, 2026-08-11, purely as context for why the shell-profile rule is easy to breach; nothing in the findings asserts a Senzing fact.
- **Summary:** Suite green at baseline (**1587 passed, 3 skipped, 1253 subtests**). **Three findings, two written as specs and one recorded here.** (1) **INV-050's layout tree enumerates three artifacts nothing produces** — `config/session_log.jsonl`, `config/visualization_tracker.json`, `docs/completion_summary.md` appear nowhere in the repo outside the invariant, so a foundational MUST has never been satisfied; spec proposes annotate-or-build plus a tree-walk guard, and deliberately leaves the annotate-vs-build call to the maintainer. (2) **The 🚨 "never modify the user's global shell configuration" guarantee at `module-02-sdk-setup/SKILL.md:502` is registered in no invariant and asserted by no test** — the plugin's only rule about files *outside* the generated project, and the most likely way to breach it is by acting on install guidance the plugin itself relays; spec drafts an invariant for sign-off. (3) **Three invariants are enforced by tests that never cite them** — INV-183 (`test_generated_html_deliverables.py`), INV-188 (`test_tab_set_is_singular.py`), INV-191 (`test_partial_row_and_schema_coverage.py`); each invariant names its test, the test exists, and `test_tab_set_is_singular.py::test_server_and_contract_agree` was read and does enforce INV-188's subject. The guarantee is real; only the back-citation is missing, which is why `coverage_reports.py invariants` counts them as unenforced. Recorded here rather than specced — the fix is three docstring lines. **Verified correct and not re-checked next run:** all three shipped slash commands are documented (`docs/README.md`); INV-197's binding set is genuinely just the free-data catalog, covered at both recommendation sites (no incomplete application); the tests named inside INV-183/188/191 all exist (no phantom-test defect); `citations.py verify` clean. **Coverage limits:** the conversational invariants INV-005-INV-009 and every gate-ordering rule were **not tested** and cannot be — they need `dry-run` phase 3. `podman` and Apple `container` are absent here, so INV-195's non-Docker dispatch is unexercised. The 15 hard-rule hits from `conformance.py rules` were **triaged, not exhaustively read** — one resolved to finding (2); the rest read as local instructions or already-covered rules, but that judgement was made on the quoted line plus surrounding context, not on a full read of all 11 files. Concision was measured (42 files, ~114k words, 129 repeated passages across 91 pairs) and **not audited** — no drifted-repetition check was performed. **Establishes no new invariant.** The run was report-before-change: finding (2) *drafts* an invariant inside its spec for the maintainer's sign-off, and it is recorded there rather than here, because an invariant is registered by `implement-spec` after approval — never by the audit that proposed it.
- **Commit:** `779e9b9`

(2) A second bullet states the flag **does** apply to `export_json_entity_report` and `export_csv_entity_report`, and that it carries `depends_on` so it produces output only when relationships are already included — "a dependency, not an exclusion". One of those assertions was itself vacuous on the first attempt: `test_the_claim_carries_its_provenance` matched the whole file, and the page carries other stamps, so stripping **this** claim's provenance still passed; it is now scoped to the paragraph and the mutation is caught. ## sdk-guide-configure-now-leads-with-seeding

- **Implemented:** 2026-08-11
- **Files changed:** `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/module-02-sdk-setup/SKILL.md`, `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/module-05-data-quality-mapping/phase3-test-load.md`, `tests/test_config_seeding_guidance.py`, `specs/sdk-guide-configure-now-leads-with-seeding.md` (deviation note)
- **MCP re-check:** **changed — the spec's explanation was wrong. Server 1.32.8, docs index 2026-08-11 13:35 UTC.** The spec said the primary/alternative roles "have swapped"; they do not. `sdk_guide(topic='configure')` **selects** by whether `data_sources` was passed: without it, primary = `python/configuration/init_default_config.py` and `register_data_sources` is an alternative; with `data_sources=["CUSTOMERS","WATCHLIST"]`, they reverse. That is the INV-169 trap — the spec called it one way and generalised. `explain_error_code('SENZ7221')` gave it away: its first resolution step now reads "see sdk_guide topic='configure' **(called WITHOUT data_sources, this returns the seeding snippet)**". The `generate_scaffold` half **is** confirmed changed: called with the exact parameters the plugin's ⚠️ block named, it returns the four `python/configuration/` snippets. Tools called: `get_capabilities`, `sdk_guide(topic='configure', language='python')` both with and without `data_sources`, `generate_scaffold(language='python', workflow='initialize')`, `explain_error_code('SENZ7221')`, `explain_error_code('SENZ0031')`.
- **Summary:** **All six criteria hold.** (1) `module-02-sdk-setup/SKILL.md:1021-1043` no longer says "take the `alternatives` entry" — it states the `data_sources` discriminator as a two-row table and instructs locating the snippet by `code.source_path` ending `configuration/init_default_config.py`, never by position. (2) The ⚠️ "does not do this" block is gone, replaced at `:1061-1066` by the corrected statement that `generate_scaffold(workflow='initialize')` reaches the same code, with `sdk_guide` preferred because only it carries `compatibility_notes`. (3) Re-verification clause satisfied — both calls re-asked this session; the deviation records what came back. (4) Three assertions in `tests/test_config_seeding_guidance.py` repointed with INV-181 docstrings: `test_it_points_at_the_configure_response_alternatives` → `test_it_explains_which_call_puts_the_snippet_where` (now requires the with/without `data_sources` discriminator), `test_the_wrong_route_is_explicitly_ruled_out` → `test_the_snippet_is_located_by_source_path_not_by_position`, and the provenance assertion relaxed from the literal `1.32.2` to any version+date — pinning an exact version made a *correct* re-verification fail, which is the anti-pattern INV-181 names. (5) The `generate-scaffold-initialize-does-not-seed` ledger row was superseded by an appended `retire-workaround` row earlier the same day. (6) `<chosen_language>` is preserved throughout; no language privileged. Suite **1584 ran, 1581 passed, 3 skipped**. **Deviations recorded:** the swapped-roles explanation corrected to parameter-dependent selection; the spec's `SENZ0031` citation is wrong (`explain_error_code('SENZ0031')` returns `EAS_ERR_INVALID_VALUE_OF_MAX_DEGREE`; the code at that line is `SENZ7221`); `phase3-test-load.md:49-51` repeated the `alternatives` claim **and** said `SENZ7221`'s own guidance "does not name the cause", now false — both corrected, the second beyond what the spec anticipated; and two things the spec did not know existed are now relayed rather than asserted (the `compatibility_notes` precondition block, and `explain_error_code('SENZ7221')`'s own cause and remedy). **Establishes no new invariant** — INV-080 and INV-169 already govern; this is their application.
- **Commit:** `ec1d1d6`

The upstream residual named in the spec was sent 2026-08-11 as `category='bug'` with the maintainer's explicit approval (`list_files` advertises a `file_path` route that elides); anonymous, so no reply is possible. **Establishes no new invariant** — INV-160 is corrected in place, as the maintainer directed.
