## reframe-the-quiz-as-a-knowledge-check

- **Implemented:** 2026-08-11
- **Files changed:** `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/module-00-entity-resolution-concepts/concepts.md`, `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/module-00-entity-resolution-concepts/SKILL.md`, `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/docs/examples/bootcamp_recap.example.md` + `.pdf` (regenerated), `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/docs/model-selection.md`, `tests/test_phase3_interaction_prose.py`, `tests/test_example_recap_sync.py`, `tests/test_recap_pdf_guard.py`, `specs/reframe-the-quiz-as-a-knowledge-check.md` (deviation + no-invariant notes)
- **MCP re-check:** **n/a (no Senzing fact); no tool called, and no server version stamped.** Module 0's offer wording, its heading and the recap vocabulary are entirely plugin-side, as the spec's own Source section recorded. Stated rather than skipped per INV-080. No Senzing fact was copied from the spec into shipped text; the knowledge-check questions themselves remain MCP-sourced at run time, and that rule is untouched.
- **Summary:** **Seven criteria hold; the eighth is the spec's own pre-declared disclosure.** (1) `concepts.md` no longer frames the exercise as a test in its heading (`:113`), its prose, or the pinned question — the single surviving "quiz" is the new rule at `:133` naming the words to avoid, which is a mention rather than a use, and the test exempts exactly that line. (2) The pinned question is **"Would you like a few quick questions to help the concepts stick?"** (maintainer-chosen from three drafts) — names the gain, one `?`, no "or", asserted against INV-008/INV-009/INV-051. (3) Pinned verbatim in one authoritative place (INV-056); INV-112's every-run guarantee is unchanged and the `quiz_offered` / `quiz_taken` progress keys were deliberately left alone as internal state, pinned by `test_the_progress_keys_are_deliberately_unchanged`. (4) **Verified by opening `bootcamp_recap.example.md`** (INV-182): `:32` quotes the current question, `:43` and `:52` use the new vocabulary, and the PDF was regenerated with the documented command — `test_example_recap_sync` passes, so it is still reproducible from its source (INV-065). (5) See the deviation: no test asserted the old wording, so instead of repointing nothing, a class pinning the *requirement* was added. (6) The mechanism is untouched — count, difficulty, sourcing and the wrong-answer handling from `dry-run-phase3-interaction-prose-defects` all still pass their existing assertions. (7) **Not runtime-verified, as the spec required:** whether the new framing actually reduces declines is a conversational outcome, and one `dry-run` phase-3 walk would not establish it. (8) Markdown plus stdlib-only tests — Linux/macOS/Windows and language-agnostic. Suite **1584 ran, 1581 passed, 3 skipped** (was 1577; +7). **Five mutations were tried and all five caught**: old wording restored (2 failures), heading reverted (1), softened to "explore the concepts further?" (1), an "or" introduced (1), reason note deleted (2). **Deviations recorded** in the spec: criterion 5 was vacuous as written, and meeting it properly gave **INV-112 its first test citation in the suite**; regenerating the example PDF shifted pagination onto a page boundary and broke `test_example_recap_sync` on a line that is genuinely present — the page-number footer welded into its 50-character comparison window — so the assertion's model of the artifact was corrected rather than the artifact (INV-181), with mutation proof that staleness is still caught; three files beyond the Affected list carried the same vocabulary (`SKILL.md`, `docs/model-selection.md`, a recap fixture) and were updated so one word is not stated two ways; and several lines were re-wrapped because "knowledge check" is longer than "quiz". **Establishes no new invariant** — confirmed with the maintainer: INV-056 already pins the wording in one place and INV-112 already guarantees it is asked, so the framing adds no standing rule.
- **Commit:** `b0af556`

## readiness-gate-acts-on-structure-while-naming-semantics

- **Implemented:** 2026-08-11
- **Files changed:** `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/module-05-data-quality-mapping/phase1-quality-assessment.md`, `tests/test_fastpath_gates_on_full_mapping.py` (new), `specs/INVARIANTS.md` (INV-198 + its index entry + the in-place note on INV-040), `specs/readiness-gate-acts-on-structure-while-naming-semantics.md` (deviation + invariant notes)
- **MCP re-check:** **confirmed and extended, server 1.32.8, docs index 2026-08-11 13:35 UTC** (spec was written against 1.32.3, 2026-07-31). Three calls, each changing what shipped: `get_sample_data(dataset='las-vegas', source='PPP_LOANS')` returned records with exactly the split the spec described — 8 specification attributes, 11 raw columns — which **turned criterion 8 from a disclosure into a verified fact**, and no "Denied." filtering occurred; `search_docs(category='data_mapping')` established that the catalog names the attributes `NAME_ORG` and `ADDR_LINE1`/`ADDR_CITY` (*Feature: NAME*) while the record ships `BUSINESS_NAME_ORG`/`BUSINESS_ADDR_LINE1`, and that a usage type is "a short label that distinguishes multiple instances of the same feature" (*Usage types and payload*) — which forced an exact-match prohibition the spec never anticipated; and the live `mapping_workflow` schema confirmed the five step-3 dispositions (`feature`, `payload`, `ignore`, `derived`, `extract`), which is what makes routing undecided columns to mapping the correct answer rather than a cop-out. Tools called: `get_capabilities`, `get_sample_data`, `search_docs` (x3), `mapping_workflow` (schema).
- **Summary:** **All nine criteria hold; none is left unverified.** (1) The result is now two fields — `senzing_loadable` (structural, `phase1-quality-assessment.md:96-114`) and `fully_mapped` with `unmapped_fields` (`:149-204`) — and the offer at `:206` is gated on **both**; `test_no_path_presents_the_offer_on_the_structural_result_alone` locates the offer's nearest gate and asserts it is the coverage result, and `test_the_checks_run_before_the_offer_in_that_order` pins the ordering, since a coverage check running after the offer would read identically to a grep. (2) A source with any unrecognised key routes to mapping at `:222-236`, as a statement not a question, with "Name every unrecognised column" and the eleven `PPP_LOANS` columns as the worked example. (3) The threshold and its full reasoning are at `:178-196` — a count not a proportion, why not a percentage, why payload-worthy columns are not excluded, and why fully-mapped sources still fast-path unchanged. (4) The all-pre-mapped case is handled at `:244-262`: it names what the bootcamper would miss and offers a raw source or the free-data catalog, recording a decline so graduation is not silent about it. (5) **Verified by opening the file** (INV-182): both shapes still admitted at `:79-94` (INV-145) and CORD-only gating intact at `:69` and item 8, `:241-242`. (6) **Verified by opening the file:** `getRecordPreview` remains optional and non-blocking at `:116-149` with the `SENZ2207` registration caveat unchanged; `test_record_preview_is_not_promoted_to_the_gate` slices the region between the coverage check and the offer to confirm it is referenced only as the optional route. (7) `tests/test_fastpath_gates_on_full_mapping.py`, 31 tests, and **five mutations were tried and all five caught**: deleting the coverage check (13 failures), re-gating the offer on structure alone (2), softening the threshold to 80% (1), removing the all-pre-mapped branch (5), and counting columns instead of naming them (1). (8) Criterion 8 verified — see the MCP re-check. (9) Markdown prose plus a stdlib-only test (`pathlib`, `re`) — Linux/macOS/Windows and language-agnostic. Suite **1577 ran, 1574 passed, 3 skipped** (was 1546; +31). **Deviations recorded** in the spec: criterion 3's premise was **inverted with the maintainer's agreement** — payload-worthy columns cannot be excluded, so they are counted as undecided and routed to the step that dispositions them; the threshold decision the spec left open was made (count, not proportion) with its reasoning at the step; and an exact-match hazard the spec did not anticipate was ruled out, with the honest limit marked — the indexed specification documents usage types but not the prefix *encoding* on a flat attribute name, so that is labelled an observed shape (INV-080/INV-149). Two coherence edits went beyond the Affected files list, both inside the same file: Step 5's categorization now defers the routing decision to Step 5a instead of stating it a second way, and the `fast_path_reason` example plus one "Senzing-ready" reference were moved to the new vocabulary. **Establishes INV-198** (maintainer-approved wording) and **partly supersedes INV-040's parenthetical** — "CORD data does not require mapping nor transformation" is true of the `truthset` class it was written for and false of `PPP_LOANS`. The conflict was surfaced to the maintainer before anything was recorded; INV-040 carries an in-place note, and its main clause plus INV-041/042's fast-pathed-source exemptions are unchanged.
- **Commit:** `33c7259`

(2) It states the cost of using it: "You do not lose your place — the note is saved under `docs/feedback/`, then the bootcamp question you were on comes straight back", which is exactly what INV-074's exit banner does per `ground-rules.md:436-443`. (3) It is a statement — `test_it_is_a_statement_not_a_question` asserts no line in step 3 begins with 👉 — and the `concise` form is pinned to a single line at `:145-146`. (7) **Not runtime-verified, as the spec required:** whether a bootcamper who was told actually uses the phrase is a conversational outcome only `dry-run` phase 3 can observe; nothing static can reach it. That is the identical qualification INV-105 already carries for the plugin-version line eight lines above it in the same file. **Establishes INV-196** (maintainer-approved wording), indexed under **Feedback capture** — INV-010 guaranteed the capability at any time, but nothing required it to be announced, which is why a control the guide knew about was reachable only by bootcampers told out of band.

## container-lifecycle-hooks-assume-docker

- **Implemented:** 2026-08-11
- **Files changed:** `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/scripts/docker_lifecycle.py`, `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/scripts/session-end.py` (docstring), `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/module-02-sdk-setup/SKILL.md`, `tests/test_container_lifecycle_runtimes.py` (new), `specs/INVARIANTS.md` (INV-195), `specs/container-lifecycle-hooks-assume-docker.md` (deviation + invariant notes)
- **MCP re-check:** **n/a (no Senzing fact); no tool called, and no server version stamped.** The spec asserts nothing Senzing owns — a progress-file key, a container-CLI probe, and a hook message are entirely plugin-side, as the spec's own Source section recorded. Stated rather than skipped per INV-080. Disclosure: the Senzing MCP server was **not reachable this session** (only the `authenticate` stub is exposed, no `get_capabilities`), so even a courtesy version stamp was unavailable; nothing in this change needed one.
- **Summary:** **Eight criteria hold; the ninth is the spec's own pre-declared disclosure.** (1) Both Module 2 registration sites now record the `runtime` alongside `name`, **verified by opening the file** (INV-182) rather than inferred: `module-02-sdk-setup/SKILL.md:382-391` (the container SDK-install route) and `:832-835` (the PostgreSQL container) — note the line drift from the spec's `:383`/`:827`, since the first block grew from 5 lines to 10. (2) `docker_lifecycle.py` replaces the single `docker_available()` probe with `runtime_cli(runtime)` resolving a per-runtime CLI, and `stop_started_containers()` issues `[<resolved-cli>, "stop", <name>]` per entry. (3) `resume_summary()` groups entries by recorded runtime and names each CLI in backticks; the old "This bootcamp uses Docker container(s): " string is gone. (4) A dict entry with no `runtime` and a bare name string both resolve to `docker`, so existing progress files are untouched — `test_dict_entry_without_runtime_is_docker`, `test_bare_string_entry_is_docker`, `test_legacy_entry_resolves_to_docker_runtime`. (5) An absent CLI runs **no** command, stops nothing, and is reported by name; both hooks exit 0 end-to-end against a genuinely-absent runtime — `AbsentCliWarnsAndContinues`, `HooksExitCleanly` (INV-101/INV-052). (6) The `docker_containers` key is unchanged, with the reason ("would break every in-flight bootcamp's progress file for a cosmetic gain") recorded in the module docstring, at Module 2 `:390-391`, and pinned by `test_progress_key_is_still_docker_containers`, which asserts a `containers` key is ignored. (7) All four required cases covered, 18 tests in `tests/test_container_lifecycle_runtimes.py`. (8) **Not runtime-verified, as the spec required:** Apple's `container` CLI is absent on this Linux machine, so dispatch to it is asserted at the command-construction level with a stubbed CLI lookup, never against the real binary. Every test stubs both `shutil.which` and `subprocess.run` — mandatory here, since this machine has a real `docker` and an unstubbed test would stop real containers. (9) Stdlib-only, `shutil.which` for CLI resolution, exec-form hooks, no shell — Linux/macOS/Windows and language-agnostic (INV-001/002/052). Suite **1524 ran, 1521 passed, 3 skipped** (was 1503 passed + 18 new). **Deviations recorded** in the spec: state probing is restricted to runtimes with a verified `ps -a --filter --format` interface (`docker`, `podman`) and any other reports `unknown`, rather than guessing at Apple's unverified list syntax; the runtime set is a closed allowlist so a progress-file value can never become a binary a hook invokes (`test_unknown_runtime_is_never_executed`); `docker_available()` was removed outright after a grep confirmed no consumer outside the module; and the spec's `_load()` `:55-59` citation is drift — the append is `tracked_containers()` at `:53`, though the claim it supported was correct. **Establishes INV-195** (maintainer-approved wording), generalising INV-101, whose Docker-only text assumed the single runtime available when it was written.
- **Commit:** `acda007`

## mapping-workflow-step1-script-defects

- **Implemented:** 2026-07-31
- **Files changed:** `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/module-05-data-quality-mapping/phase2-data-mapping.md`, `tests/test_verbatim_check_limitation.py`, `specs/mapping-workflow-step1-script-defects.md` (deviation note)
- **MCP re-check:** **server 1.32.3, 2026-07-31 — prescriptions confirmed; the defects deliberately not re-run.** The live `mapping_workflow` schema declares the `derived` disposition with its `derived_as` enum (`DATA_SOURCE`, `RECORD_ID`, `RECORD_TYPE`, `REL_ANCHOR`, `REL_POINTER`) and `type_discriminator` with `field_overrides` as a step-3 field — which is what makes the counter's double error a defect rather than a preference. Reproducing the three needs a live workflow run with a workspace and multi-file/headerless sources, which this environment did not do. Tools called: `get_capabilities`, `mapping_workflow` (schema).
- **Summary:** **All nine criteria hold; one is a disclosure rather than a tick.** (1) The field-count warning is documented at `### 11. Map` as expected-under-stated-conditions, with the counting rule (`derived` counted though not source fields; `type_discriminator.field_overrides` excluded though they are) and the observed 12→13 / 16→14 figures; the guidance directs the reader to confirm every field carries a disposition and explicitly refuses to teach ignoring the step's other warnings. (2) Multi-file profiling is instructed to `profile_report_<stem>.md` with the silent-overwrite reason — "only the second file's profile survives … the file exists, is well-formed, and describes one schema". (3)+(4) The headerless-CSV limitation, its headered-copy workaround and the sentinel-token caveat are all at `### 9. Profile`, where the profiler runs (INV-183), with INV-128 cross-referenced. (5) All three are marked "reported upstream 2026-07-31" and "not re-run since", the retirable shape `:342` uses. (6) The ⛔ against shipping a patched profiler is stated with INV-173. (7) `Step1ProfilerLimitationsAreDocumentedAtTheirSteps` adds 8 tests asserting **placement** as well as presence. (8) **Not runtime-verified, and pinned:** the text presents all three as dated field reports from 2026-07-27 on SDK 4.3.3.26191. (9) Prose plus stdlib-only tests. Suite **1503 passed, 3 skipped, 1183 subtests** (was 1495). **Deviations recorded:** the three went to **two** steps rather than one block — grouping justified one spec, not one location, and INV-183 puts a rule where it fires; the sentinel caveat was placed by consequence (where population percentages are reported) rather than under "headerless CSV", since `-0-` has nothing to do with headers and filing it there would hide it from every non-headerless source. All eleven mutations were caught first time, breaking this session's pattern — deliberately, because the guards assert consequences ("only the second file's profile survives", "one record disappears", "wrong in both directions") rather than topic words. One of my own tests failed on correct text first: it ran against raw section slices while the prose is line-wrapped, so a phrase assertion was really an assertion about line breaks; the slices are now flattened. Upstream state also changed between spec and implementation — the spec recorded all three as "offered at graduation" (never sent); they were sent 2026-07-31 as one `category='bug'` report with approval. **Establishes no new invariant** — INV-173 and INV-128 already govern; this records three instances at their steps.

- **Implemented:** 2026-07-31
- **Files changed:** `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/graduation/SKILL.md`, `specs/INVARIANTS.md`, `tests/test_graduation_announces_what_it_produces.py` (new), `specs/step5b-keepsake-pdfs-are-never-announced.md` (deviation note)
- **MCP re-check:** **n/a (no Senzing fact).** Every claim is internal consistency between graduation's own steps, its own renderer and its own layout invariant. No MCP tool owns any of it and none was called — stated rather than skipped per INV-080. Root cause reproduced before changing anything: `business_problem.pdf` and `data_source_evaluation.pdf` appeared in exactly two places plugin-wide, the Step 5b invocation (`graduation/SKILL.md:833`) and the renderer's docstring (`generate_document_pdf.py:19`), and in neither the closing instruction nor its example.
- **Summary:** **All eight criteria hold; every one runtime-verified.** (1) The closing step's item 2 now names both PDFs with a one-clause purpose each, in the wording Step 5b supplies. (2) Conditionality is explicit — "each only if it exists", "an absent or refused PDF is simply not named", citing item 1's confirm-before-announcing rule and INV-048; `TheAnnouncementStaysConditional` asserts all four. (3) The example blockquote models both, under its existing "Example (list only what exists)" caption so it cannot read as unconditional. (4) Step 5b's `:869` delegation now resolves, verified by `Step5bDelegationResolves::test_the_site_it_delegates_to_names_both_documents`, which reads the **closing step** rather than Step 5b (INV-182). (5) INV-050's tree names both with a dated clarification recording that they are **best-effort**, not always-produced, since Step 5b warns-and-continues; nothing deleted or renumbered. (6) `test_graduation_announces_what_it_produces.py` derives the produced set and asserts each is named in the instruction **and** the example separately; the class test — a third document added to Step 5b and left unannounced — fails, so the next artifact is covered without editing the guard. (7) `citations.py verify` clean; suite **1460 passed, 3 skipped, 1153 subtests** (was 1439). (8) Prose plus a stdlib-only test. **Deviation recorded** in the spec: the guard's first version derived from `--output` alone, returned only `docs/business_problem.pdf` — because Step 5b shows one worked invocation and names the second document only as `.md` in prose — and therefore **passed while `data_source_evaluation.pdf` was dropped from both sites**, verified by mutation. That is a guard narrower than the property it claims, the third instance of that class in one day. Fixed by unioning `--output` targets with Step 5b's own source-document list mapped `.md` → `.pdf`, with a ≥2 floor so a narrowing fails loudly. A second, smaller instance: the presence check spanned the whole closing section, which contains the example, so dropping an artifact from the instruction alone passed — instruction and example are now sliced apart. Eight mutations then all caught. **Establishes no new invariant** — it builds the second consumer an existing criterion already required and records what the flow produces; the durable half is the class test.

## inv121-cursor-discipline-is-guarded-in-one-generator

- **Implemented:** 2026-07-31
- **Files changed:** `specs/INVARIANTS.md`, `tests/test_recap_pdf_text_column.py` (new), `specs/inv121-cursor-discipline-is-guarded-in-one-generator.md` (deviation note)
- **MCP re-check:** **n/a (no Senzing fact).** Cursor position in a bundled PDF generator is plugin-internal; no MCP tool owns it and none was called. Root cause confirmed before changing anything: `tests/test_discoveries_pdf.py` is the only test mentioning cursor position and imports `generate_discoveries_pdf` only, while INV-121 binds "a bundled generator".
- **Summary:** **Seven criteria hold; one holds narrowly and is disclosed rather than ticked broadly.** (1) `tests/test_recap_pdf_text_column.py` asserts cursor discipline positionally for `generate_recap_pdf.py` — the fixture puts a table immediately before full-width prose, which is the exact hazard INV-121 names (`multi_cell` leaves the cursor at the right margin), and asserts each marked run's start x is in the page's left half, the threshold `test_discoveries_pdf.py` calibrated against a defect that drew at x≈567 pt on a 595 pt page. **⚠️ Path-scoped:** the module-prose writer (`:2605`) is negative-controlled; injecting the same defect at the code-block writer (`:2459`) and its rule (`:2462`) **escaped**, so those paths, the cover, the TOC, image alt text and the stdlib fallback are **not** covered. Said in the test's docstring and in INV-121's note; claiming "every full-width write" would have reproduced the defect this spec exists to fix. (2) The certificate page **is** rendered and covered — three runs on it, and the negative-x check spans it; the certificate is landscape at 841 pt against the body's 595 pt, so a single page-width bound would have false-failed. (3) `pdf_generators()` globs `generate_*_pdf.py` with a ≥2 not-vacuous floor. (4) `generate_document_pdf.py` excluded explicitly, plus a test that it still delegates — if it grows drawing code the exclusion stops being safe and the suite says so. (5) Negative control satisfied at `:2605`, with the mutation confirmed to have landed (compiles, line verified) before the result was believed. (6) INV-121's enforcement clause now names both tests as a dated in-place clarification stating no meaning change; the requirement is untouched and nothing was deleted or renumbered. (7) `tests/test_discoveries_pdf.py` passes **unchanged** — `git diff --stat` empty, 53 passed. (8) Skips rather than passes when `fpdf2` is absent, with the reason named (INV-163). **Deviation recorded:** reaching that answer required distinguishing a mis-targeted mutation from an escaped one four times — removing all seven `set_x(pdf.l_margin)` calls escaped because none is on a path the fixture exercises, and injecting into `add_wrapped` escaped because that name at `:2834` is a nested function in the **stdlib fallback**, not the fpdf2 renderer under test. Each looked identical to a broken guard. **Predicted-file accounting:** the spec's `## Affected files` offered `tests/` as "a **new file, or** an added class in `tests/test_recap_pdf_certificate.py`". The new-file route was taken, so **`tests/test_recap_pdf_certificate.py` is deliberately unchanged** — its scope is the certificate page and this guard checks the whole document's body flow, so an added class there would have mixed two subjects; its existing `TheFixedPageCannotBeOverrun` already covers the certificate's own bounds and still passes. Recorded because the ledger's affected-files gate caught the silence — correctly — rather than because the choice was wrong. **Establishes no new invariant** — it extends an existing rule's enforcement to the rest of the class it already bound.
- **Commit:** `fd71055`

## inert-screenshot-omission-conflicts-with-embed-every-tab

- **Implemented:** 2026-07-31
- **Files changed:** `specs/INVARIANTS.md`, `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/bootcamp-onboarding/module-completion.md`, `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/graduation/SKILL.md`, `tests/test_screenshot_retention_and_order.py`, `tests/test_invariant_crossreferences.py`, `specs/inert-screenshot-omission-conflicts-with-embed-every-tab.md` (deviation note)
- **MCP re-check:** **n/a (no Senzing fact).** Both invariants govern the plugin's own capture and recap apparatus — an image, a caption and a backfill. No MCP tool owns either claim and none was called; stated rather than skipped per INV-080.
- **Summary:** **All eight criteria hold; every one runtime-verified.** (1) INV-123's operative clause now reads "the caption MUST say so" with the `or the image MUST be omitted` disjunction removed; `test_inv123_offers_no_omission` asserts it against the *operative* text, and `test_inv123_still_requires_the_caption_to_say_so` pins the surviving requirement. (2) INV-123 cites INV-146 as the reason omission is unavailable and INV-146 records that it closed the branch; `test_inv123_points_at_inv146` and `test_inv146_records_that_it_closed_the_branch` assert both directions, and `test_inv146_is_unchanged_in_substance` confirms closing the branch did not relax INV-146. (3) Dated in-place clarifications on both, each stating no meaning change (the maintainer ruled this mechanism on 2026-07-31); nothing deleted or renumbered. (4) **Both consumers opened and checked, not inferred** (INV-182): `module-completion.md:248-258` now requires the inert caption and carries a ⛔ block stating omission is not an alternative; `graduation/SKILL.md:431-443` was already correct on completeness ("**all** of them, not a 'best' few") and gained the inertness disclosure. (5) `OmittingAnInertCaptureIsNotAnOption` sweeps all shipped markdown for an offered omission and both call sites for the bare form, with positive controls (the exact removed clauses) and negative controls (four benign phrases). (6) **No test pinned the omission branch** — verified by grepping `tests/` for the removed wording, which returns nothing; that absence is *why* it shipped, since the existing sweep looked for count caps and tab order only. Nothing to repoint. (7) `citations.py verify` clean. (8) Prose plus stdlib-only tests. **Deviation recorded** in the spec: the unverified half resolved as a **live defect** (`module-completion.md:250` shipped the disjunction verbatim) but with a different mechanism than predicted — omitting does **not** lose the image, because graduation's backfill embeds every unreferenced PNG; what is lost is the caption, since the backfill captions from the tab slug and knows nothing about inertness. So following INV-123 produced exactly what INV-123 forbids. One change beyond the criteria, in a file the spec did list: the backfill now discloses an empty/inactive search state, because it runs precisely when the capture step's caption never happened. Guards were written badly twice — the first sweep regex was simultaneously too broad (firing on INV-162's required "reports every one it drops") and too narrow (missing "or omit it") — and three mutations escaped, two of them real gaps: the ruleset text had no guard at all (the sweep reads `plugins/`), and downgrading the ⛔ gate to a note passed every absence check. Both now asserted. **Establishes no new invariant** — it removes a permission INV-146 had already voided and writes down a boundary; no new standing rule arises.
- **Commit:** `3818597`

## invariants-index-flattens-partial-supersession

- **Implemented:** 2026-07-31
- **Files changed:** `specs/INVARIANTS.md`, `tests/test_invariants_index.py`, `specs/invariants-index-flattens-partial-supersession.md` (deviation note)
- **MCP re-check:** **n/a (no Senzing fact).** Nothing in this change asserts anything about Senzing — it is the classification of the plugin's own ruleset — so no MCP tool owns any claim here and none was called. Stated rather than skipped per INV-080's discipline.
- **Summary:** **All nine criteria hold; every one runtime-verified.** (1) The index now carries two labelled sublists, `*Fully superseded — skip these…*` and `*Partly superseded — still binding; read the invariant:*`; `test_the_two_labels_give_opposite_instructions` asserts every occurrence of the first says "skip" and every occurrence of the second says "read" and never "skip". (2) INV-079, INV-086 and INV-137 are in the partial sublist, each with an inline note naming the replaced clause and its replacement (`INVARIANTS.md:264`, `:277`, `:272`). (3) INV-063 and INV-069 moved out of the skip list into the same sublist, each marked **restored** and naming INV-137 as what reinstated it. (4) No invariant statement was touched — `git diff` on this change shows edits only inside `### Index by subject`; the fully/partly split is metadata. (5) Both counts removed from the index prose **and** from this test file's docstring (which carried "142 development rules", "4,614 citations", "22 superseded" — all drifted); `test_no_count_is_pinned_in_the_index_prose` fails if any returns, and asserts its own prose slice is non-vacuous first. (6) `test_each_appears_in_exactly_one_group` and `test_no_invariant_is_missing_from_the_index` still pass with both sublists counted as indexed — 13 groups, 144 IDs, matching INV-051–194 exactly. (7) `test_nothing_is_marked_fully_superseded_that_is_only_partly` plus `test_the_full_list_matches_what_the_invariants_declare` / `…partial_list…` compare the index against what each invariant says about *itself*, replacing an `actual()` that matched any occurrence of "superseded by INV" and therefore could not tell a clause-level supersession from a total one. (8) `citations.py verify` clean (194 defined, every citation and Source resolving). (9) Prose and stdlib-only tests, no platform or language dependency. **Deviation recorded** in the spec's `## Deviations from this spec, and why (2026-07-31)`: the spec named five invariants, the classifier found **six** — INV-104 is partly superseded by INV-155 on its tab enumeration while listed fully *live*, the mirror image of the reported defect, now in the partial sublist and pinned by `test_a_partly_superseded_rule_is_never_also_listed_as_live`. Final counts are 8 fully superseded and 6 partly-superseded-or-restored, not the 13-and-0 the index claimed. Five mutations caught (INV-137 or INV-079 back under "skip", INV-104 dropped from the partial list, a count creeping back, a label inverted); one initially escaped and was a real weakness — the label test used `next()` and read only the first of three partly-superseded lines, now iterating all under `subTest`. Three of my own bugs were caught by the tests as I added them, all recorded in the deviation note. **Establishes no new invariant** — this corrects the *classification* of existing invariants and removes two drifted counts; every rule it touches already said of itself what the index now records. The durable guarantee is carried by the tests rather than by a new ID.
- **Commit:** `e53fece`

## separate-server-owned-commands-from-plugin-owned-update-checks

- **Implemented:** 2026-07-31
- **Files changed:** `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/module-02-sdk-setup/SKILL.md` (Step 1b preamble and all four per-platform command blocks), `tests/test_sdk_update_offer.py`, `specs/separate-server-owned-commands-from-plugin-owned-update-checks.md`
- **MCP re-check:** **changed — this spec's own ownership table was wrong.** Server 1.32.2, docs indexed 2026-07-29 11:11 UTC, 2026-07-31. Re-asked `sdk_guide(topic='install', …)` for `macos_arm` (and re-read the `linux_apt`/`linux_yum`/`windows` responses from the sweep) and read the **whole** payload — `install_commands`, all `gotchas`, `post_install`, `anti_patterns`. The table lists `brew upgrade --cask` as "yes — `install_commands`"; it **appears nowhere**. macOS documents `brew tap`, `brew trust`, `brew install --cask`, `brew uninstall --cask`/`brew untap` only. Windows documents `scoop bucket add` and `scoop install`, not `scoop update`. So **on macOS and Windows the update command itself is plugin-owned**, and only apt/yum update via the same command the server documents. Also re-confirmed the spec's re-verification clause: no installed-version query and no available-version check on any of the four platforms.
- **Summary:** Criterion walk. (1) The ambiguous sentence at `:132` is replaced by a two-bullet split with explicit precedence — server-documented commands are "on loan — re-read it, do not trust the copy below", and "**the response wins**"; the inlined forms are a dated illustration (`:134-140`). (2) The plugin-owned half states the server documents "**no version query and no update check on any of the four platforms**", so a reader who fails to find them "that is expected, not an error" (`:141-146`). (3) The two groups are distinguishable **inside** the fences, not only in the preamble — four in-fence labels, asserted individually by `test_the_labels_are_inside_the_command_blocks_not_only_the_preamble`, plus the yum prose form which needed its own marking since it has no fence. (4) **No command was deleted** — `test_no_inlined_command_was_deleted` iterates all ten as subTests, and mutation M8 (deleting `brew outdated --cask`) is caught. (5) The server-owned half keeps its tool/version/date provenance. (6) ⚠️ **This criterion needed writing, not checking** — nothing in Step 1b carried the caveat. It is now stated in actionable form: the `brew`/`scoop` forms are exercised on Linux only, so *read what the command printed* rather than trusting its exit status, which is INV-163's discipline applied to a command rather than a check, and which strengthens the existing zero-exit-code warning instead of duplicating it. (7) `tests/test_sdk_update_offer.py` passes — 48 tests, 29 subtests, with a new 12-test `CommandOwnershipIsDistinguished` class. (8) Re-verification clause satisfied above. (9) Pure Markdown guidance, no platform or language dependency. Nine guards **mutation-tested and reverted**, including restoring the original ambiguous sentence, dropping "the response wins", and **claiming `brew upgrade --cask` is server-documented — i.e. reintroducing this spec's own error**, which the new test catches. **Deviations recorded** in the spec's `## Deviations from this spec, and why (2026-07-31)`: the `brew upgrade --cask` correction and the resulting per-platform asymmetry table; criterion 6 requiring new text; and a process note — **five of nine mutations first reported a missing target rather than a passing test** because `\n` in `python3 -c` argv is a literal backslash-n and because I guessed line wrapping instead of reading it. Re-run correctly, 9 of 9 fail as they should. That is the **third time this session** a missing target has masqueraded as an escaped mutation, and it is worth checking before believing a guard failed. **No new invariant** — INV-080, INV-163 and INV-194 already applied; this makes the plugin's own text conform to them. Full suite: **1355 passed, 3 skipped, 1076 subtests** (from 1343).
- **Commit:** 8ba1b5c

## offer-to-update-an-existing-senzing-install

- **Implemented:** 2026-07-31
- **Files changed:** `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/module-02-sdk-setup/SKILL.md` (new Step 1b, and the V4.0+ branch that routes into it), `tests/test_sdk_update_offer.py` (new), `specs/offer-to-update-an-existing-senzing-install.md`
- **MCP re-check:** **changed — the spec's central platform claim was wrong.** Server 1.32.2, docs indexed 2026-07-29 11:11 UTC, 2026-07-31. Tools called: `get_capabilities` (`senzing_version` is the string `"current"`, so it cannot answer "what is latest"); `sdk_guide(topic='install', …)` for `linux_apt`, `linux_yum`, `macos_arm`, `windows`; `search_docs(query='upgrade Senzing to a newer version existing installation')` (V3→V4 migration only — no 4.x→4.y procedure); `get_sdk_reference(topic='response_schemas', filter='get_version')` (empty `data`; `SzProduct.get_version()` returns a plain string whose shape is undocumented — INV-149). The spec said availability is Linux-only and macOS/Windows must report the check skipped. **False:** that conclusion came from asking `platform='macos_arm'` with `language='python'`, which returns nothing *because the Python SDK is Linux-only* — a language dead end, not a platform one. With `language='java'`, macOS returns a full Homebrew-cask path and Windows a full Scoop path. INV-194 for the third time this session.
- **Summary:** Criterion walk. (1) Step 1's V4.0+ branch now routes into **Step 1b**, which compares the installed version against what is available rather than only against the V4.0 floor — `test_the_v4_branch_routes_into_it`. (2) The dot/hyphen trap is stated and the preference ordering given: `szBuildVersion.json`'s `BUILD_VERSION` is `4.3.3.26191` where every package manager writes `4.3.3-26191`; both forms appear and a test asserts that exact pair. (3)-(4) **Four mechanisms, not one, and the package manager is the availability oracle on each** — `dpkg-query`/`apt-cache policy`, `rpm -q`/`yum check-update` (`dnf` on RHEL 8+), `brew outdated --cask`/`brew info --cask`, `scoop status`/`scoop info`, plus Docker where the image tag *is* the version. Skipping is the fallback for any platform where the version cannot be determined, and "no data" is never rendered as "up to date" (INV-163) — **satisfied differently than the spec wrote it**, see the deviation. (5) Exactly **one asked 👉 question**, asserted by counting 👉 at line start — a bare 👉 mid-sentence is prose describing the rule, and counting those made my first version of that test fail against correct content. (6) Declining keeps the install, says so in one line, records nothing as a failure, and is **not re-asked** (INV-006) — with the outcome checkpointed (`up-to-date` / `update-declined` / `updated-to-…` / `check-skipped-…`) so a resumed session does not re-offer. (7) Latest by default; a named version uses the versioned `direct_download` URL on apt, and for Homebrew casks and Scoop the guidance **says the server documents no version-exact install** rather than inventing a pin. A test asserts `apt install pkg=version` is never claimed. (8) The EULA question reuses Step 3 Phase 2 rather than a second copy — and the **variable differs per platform** (`HOMEBREW_SENZING_ACCEPT_EULA=i_accept_the_senzing_eula`, lowercase, on macOS vs `SENZING_ACCEPT_EULA=I_ACCEPT_THE_SENZING_EULA` elsewhere), with a test pinning the lowercase because it is the detail most likely to be "tidied" away. (9) sha256 verification required before installing a downloaded package. (10) The offer states no 4.x→4.y procedure is documented and does not assert migration is unnecessary. (11) Step 4 re-runs after an update, with a platform artifact probe because exit 0 is not evidence (INV-129); on failure the working version is named and Module 2 is not marked complete. (12) Non-blocking throughout (INV-048). (13) All three platforms covered — and "holds" now means each has its own mechanism, not that two report skipped. (7) The discoveries generator is asserted, not assumed — it imports `_pdf_escape`, and its own direct-token route is rendered and checked. (9) Pure Python stdlib, no platform or language dependency. **Deviations recorded** in the spec's `## Deviations from this spec, and why (2026-07-31)`: `_safe` is called at the writers' **boundaries** rather than inside `_pdf_escape`, because the recap generator measures width on raw text by design and `∞` → `infinity` would desync every measured width and wrap decision from what is drawn; two sanitisation points per generator cover different routes (direct `add()` vs pre-`_wrap`); `test_example_recap_sync.py`'s folding helper was **not** redundant — the substitutions still happen, in `_safe` — but it hardcoded 9 of 33, the same duplicated-subset pattern being removed, so it now derives from `_UNICODE_MAP`; and **a weak test of mine was replaced** — a source-scrape for `"_safe(text)"` passed even with the boundary removed, so it is now behavioural via the title route. ## embedded-image-count-needs-an-external-denominator

- **Implemented:** 2026-07-31
- **Files changed:** `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/scripts/capture_screenshots.py`, `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/scripts/generate_recap_pdf.py`, `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/graduation/SKILL.md`, `tests/test_recap_tab_coverage.py` (new), `tests/test_capture_tabs.py`, `specs/embedded-image-count-needs-an-external-denominator.md`
- **MCP re-check:** n/a (no Senzing fact) — the capture helper, generator and `--check` are all plugin-bundled. Root cause re-confirmed in code at `generate_recap_pdf.py:712-720` and the call site (`image_embed_note(len(referenced_images))`).
- **Summary:** Gave the completeness count an **external** denominator. Criterion walk: (1) `capture_screenshots.py` gains `manifest_path`/`write_manifest`, writing `<name>-tabs.json` beside the PNGs with `captured` / `not_present` / `failed` as three distinct lists — best-effort, reported on failure, never raising (INV-122); written *before* the no-capture early returns and on the all-absent path, because a run that captured nothing is the case coverage most needs to see. (2) `--check` gains `find_tab_manifests` + `tab_coverage_problems` and exits 1 naming the missing slugs — exercised end to end: a 6-captured/4-referenced recap fails with `cross-source, search-probe`. (3) With no manifest it prints `SKIPPED: tab-coverage check` and explains why the embedded count cannot substitute (INV-163) — **and a corrupt manifest is reported then treated as absent**, so a parse failure is never a clean bill. (4) The success line states `embedded 6 of 6 images, 6 of 6 captured tabs reached the recap` — two deliberately different wordings, plus a ⛔ in `image_embed_note`'s docstring so the next reader does not reuse it for coverage. (5) Graduation Step 1a now warns on any **shortfall** (manifest preferred, PNG-count fallback), non-blocking; Step 1b carries the ⛔ that `embedded N of M` measures references, not coverage, and must not be cited as evidence — the item the spec said to ship even if the rest were deferred. All six new guards **mutation-tested and reverted**: reverting the check, dropping the SKIPPED report, silencing the corrupt-manifest report, dropping the coverage note, collapsing `failed` into `captured`, and drifting the recorded filename off the slug — each caught by its intended test. **Deviations recorded** in the spec's `## Deviations from this spec, and why (2026-07-31)`: `--check`'s comparison is **document-level, not per-section** (the recap records no section↔visualization mapping, so the per-section half is graduation Step 1a's — disclosed rather than implied); three-way rather than two-way outcome recording; manifest written before the early returns; corrupt-manifest handling. Two pre-existing tests enumerated the output directory and were scoped to `*.png`; one (`test_warns_when_a_visualization_module_has_no_image`) **pinned the wording this spec changes** — it named a check that fired only at zero — and now asserts the requirement, with the zero case asserted separately. Full suite: **1211 passed, 3 skipped, 918 subtests** (from 1188). **Predicted-file accounting:** the spec offered `tests/test_screenshot_retention_and_order.py` *or* `tests/test_recap_pdf_images.py` for the shortfall test; neither was touched. The tests went into a new dedicated `tests/test_recap_tab_coverage.py` instead, because the failure needs a fixture that spans **both** scripts — a manifest written by `capture_screenshots.py` and a recap checked by `generate_recap_pdf.py` — and neither existing file has that shape: `test_recap_pdf_images.py` covers image resolution inside the generator, and `test_screenshot_retention_and_order.py` covers capture ordering and retention. Splitting the fixture across them would have put half the guard in each. **New invariant INV-193** (maintainer-approved 2026-07-31): a count presented as evidence of completeness MUST derive its denominator from outside the artifact it measures, and a self-derived figure MUST say what it cannot detect and MUST NOT be cited as proof of completeness. Recorded in `specs/INVARIANTS.md` with the bidirectional `## Invariants introduced` note on the spec, and added to the "Generator behaviour" subject-index group. It is deliberately distinct from INV-110: a self-referential metric satisfies INV-110's make-loss-visible requirement while hiding the loss.
- **Commit:** 022c4b2

## nothing-writes-the-recap-checkpoint

- **Implemented:** 2026-07-31
- **Files changed:** `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/scripts/recap_checkpoint.py`, `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/scripts/checkpoint-tick.py` (new), `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/scripts/session-start.py`, `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/hooks/hooks.json`, `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/hooks/README.md`, `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/bootcamp-onboarding/ground-rules.md`, `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/bootcamp-onboarding/module-completion.md`, `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/graduation/SKILL.md`, `tests/test_recap_checkpoint_writer.py` (new), `specs/nothing-writes-the-recap-checkpoint.md`
- **MCP re-check:** n/a (no Senzing fact). The spec's re-triaged root cause was re-confirmed in code: `hooks.json` registers `precompact-recap.py` on PreCompact, which calls `fold_checkpoint()` and then merely *prints a reminder*.
- **Summary:** Implemented **Option A** (the spec's preferred design), with one honest limit stated up front: the spec's premise — "the file exists without model compliance", from "the hook family that already runs at step boundaries" — is not fully achievable, because **no hook event corresponds to a module step boundary** and **no hook can author narrative prose**. So creation is deterministic and the narrative stays guide-authored; what changed is that its absence is now legible and the reminder arrives in time. Criterion walk: (1) `ensure_checkpoint()` creates `docs/progress/` and a comment-only scaffold, called from a **new `UserPromptSubmit` hook** (`checkpoint-tick.py`, wired in `hooks.json` via `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}`, INV-185) — per-turn, not SessionStart, because a bootcamp becomes active *partway through* a session, by which time SessionStart has already run and found none. (2) No shipped text now claims the checkpoint is maintained by nobody: `ground-rules.md` states plainly that the plugin creates the file and the guide writes it, and that an unfilled checkpoint is the same loss as a missing one. (3) `fold_checkpoint()` reports **all four** outcomes on stderr and distinguishes `missing` from `present-but-unfilled` (INV-111) — different failures the spec's "folded anything or not" would have conflated. (4) n/a — scoped to Option B; INV-059 is now satisfied, not superseded, so nothing was appended to `INVARIANTS.md` and no new invariant is proposed. (5) `tests/test_recap_checkpoint_writer.py` (26 tests) asserts the design end to end, including the real hook script run via subprocess. (6) INV-103 untouched — `module-completion.md` step 2b unchanged, `test_recap_pdf_guard.py` still passes. **A scaffold is deliberately not foldable**: `generate_recap_pdf.py` warns when a `RECAP-CHECKPOINT` block survives into the recap, so folding an empty scaffold would append a meaningless block *and* raise a spurious graduation warning — `test_folding_a_scaffold_writes_nothing_at_all` pins that it creates no recap at all. **All reporting goes to stderr**, because `UserPromptSubmit`'s stdout is a JSON channel; mutation-proven (redirecting `_report` to stdout breaks seven tests, including the JSON parse). Six guards mutation-tested and reverted. **Deviations recorded** in the spec's `## Deviations from this spec, and why (2026-07-31)`, including two shipped texts that became *wrong* once the file normally exists and that the spec does not list: `graduation/SKILL.md`'s "if a checkpoint **remains**" (now matches a scaffold, so graduation would fold an empty block and could report an interruption that never happened) and `hooks/README.md`'s hook table. Full suite at this point: **1190 passed, 3 skipped, 910 subtests** (from 1164) — verified at commit `4cff770` itself; an earlier count of 1188/908 predates the two graduation/README pins added in the same commit.
- **Commit:** 4cff770

The inverted guard was **mutation-tested**: reverting ground-rules to "not what it takes" failed the test, and the mutation was reverted. (7) Documentation-only, no platform-specific content. **Establishes no invariant** — INV-132 was corrected in place, a clarification of fact leaving its MUST unchanged, so no new ID per `INVARIANTS.md`'s own rules. ## search-attribute-fallback-survives-a-failed-attempt

- **Implemented:** 2026-07-30
- **Files changed:** `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/scripts/senzing_viz_server.py`, `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/module-03b-truthset-visualization/visualization-api-reference.md`, `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/module-07-query-visualize-discover/phase1-query-visualize.md`, `tests/test_organization_search.py`, `specs/INVARIANTS.md`, `specs/search-attribute-fallback-survives-a-failed-attempt.md`
- **MCP re-check:** Server **1.32.2**, 2026-07-30 — **confirmed**, both facts, nothing changed. `get_capabilities` for the version; `search_docs(category='data_mapping')` returned the Entity Specification's *Name > Feature: NAME* table verbatim — `NAME_ORG` "Organization name", `NAME_FULL` "Single-field name when type (person vs org) is unknown or only a full name is provided", rule *"use `NAME_ORG` for organizations; use `NAME_FULL` only when the type is unknown or only a single field exists"* — so INV-164's premise stands unchanged. `get_sdk_reference(topic='parameters', filter='search_by_attributes', language='python')` gave `search_by_attributes(attributes: str, flags: int = SZ_SEARCH_BY_ATTRIBUTES_ALL, search_profile: str = '') -> str`, confirming `_search_one`'s 2-arg call and its `TypeError` 3-arg retry both remain valid; the response's cross-binding warning (C# takes 2 args where Python takes 3) is why the any-language wording added here is about behaviour, not call shape.
- **Summary:** Fixed the guard that foreclosed the `NAME_ORG` fallback. `Model.search`'s handler was `except Exception: if not items: return {...}` — unconditionally true on the first attribute, so any engine error on `NAME_FULL` returned before `NAME_ORG` was ever called, reinstating the INV-164 defect on the error path. Failures are now collected into a `failures` list and the loop `continue`s; the error is attached only when every candidate was attempted and `items` is still empty, naming each failed attribute and its message, with `attributes_tried` still listing failed attempts. **All eight acceptance criteria hold**, seven of them runtime-verified by `tests/test_organization_search.py` (23 tests, up from 18): the new `AFailedAttemptIsNotTheEndOfTheList` covers raise-then-hit (hit returned, both attributes tried, no `error` key), raise-then-no-match (error present, so "could not run" is never rendered as the clean no-match — INV-115), all-raise (every attribute named in the error), failed attempts still reported as tried, and a shape guard that no `return` survives in the handler (comment lines stripped first, since the comment explains the old `return`). The five named pre-existing tests pass unchanged. Any-language guidance updated in both places that state the rule, since INV-164 binds a query program in any language (INV-090/INV-124) and this defect class is exactly what propagates from the reference implementation. **One criterion is not runtime-verified**: the chip-verification inheritance is true by construction (chips verify through `GET /api/search`, i.e. the same `Model.search`) and was exercised under Node with a stubbed endpoint, but not against a live engine with loaded data, which this environment lacks — recorded in the spec's `## Deviations from this spec, and why (2026-07-30)` along with the re-verified `search_by_attributes` signature. Full suite: **1084 passed, 3 skipped, 561 subtests** (was 1070).
- **Commit:** e54fcf8

## pr7-review-minor-fixes

- **Implemented:** 2026-07-30
- **Files changed:** `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/scripts/generate_recap_pdf.py`, `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/scripts/senzing_viz_server.py`, `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/scripts/capture_screenshots.py`, `tests/test_capture_tabs.py`, `tests/test_markdown_hygiene.py`, `specs/pr7-review-minor-fixes.md`
- **MCP re-check:** n/a (no Senzing fact) — all four items are internal: a stderr diagnostic, a JS concurrency change, a Python module-global precondition, and a Markdown encoding sweep. Server 1.32.2 was contacted this session for the sibling spec; nothing here depends on it.
- **Summary:** All four items implemented; **all nine acceptance criteria hold and every one is runtime-verified.** Items 1-2 (`generate_recap_pdf.py`): the nine fallback-palette assignments were factored into `_use_fallback_palette()` mirroring `generate_discoveries_pdf.py`, then the bare `except Exception:` was split into `ModuleNotFoundError` (names the directory searched) and `except Exception as exc` (names the exception), each writing a distinct stderr line — closing the INV-111 gap where the keepsake generator degraded in total silence while its sibling reported. Verified by executing both branches through an import-blocking probe (correct message each, palette equal to `_FALLBACK_RGB`) and by running the real generator against the shipped example recap with `brand_tokens.py` removed from a copied `scripts/` tree: the warning printed, exit stayed **0**, and the PDF rendered at *99% retention, 13 of 13 images* — byte-identical in size to the normal run, since INV-107/INV-184 keep the two palettes equal. `tests/test_brand_sync.py` still passes. Item 3 (`senzing_viz_server.py` `loadProbes`): the serial `for…await` chip verification became one `Promise.all` with a per-candidate `.catch`, order and the six-chip cap reapplied after the batch. Verified by extracting the rendered page's JS and running it under Node against a stubbed engine — chips `E1,E4,E6,E7,E8,E9` in merge order (identical to the serial version's), the erroring candidate dropping only itself, ten concurrent requests, **24 ms instead of ~200 ms**; both rendered `<script>` blocks pass `node --check`. Item 4 (`capture_screenshots.py`): the `_CURRENT_TAB` comment now states the sequential-only precondition and the INV-122 consequence, plus a `_CAPTURE_IN_FLIGHT` flag that warns on stderr (never raises — INV-052/INV-048) on reentrancy and clears in a `finally`; four new tests in `tests/test_capture_tabs.py` pin the comment, the warning, the flag surviving a raising backend, and silence on a normal sequential run. Item 5: `tests/test_markdown_hygiene.py` now runs `mojibake_lines` over every shipped `.md` through the existing glob, so the same not-vacuous guard covers it. **Deviations recorded** in the spec's `## Deviations from this spec, and why (2026-07-30)`: the sweep found one legitimate hit — `ground-rules.md:226` quotes the corruption it teaches — exempted per INV-173 as narrowly as the case allows (that file, inline-code only, corrupted prose there still fails) and made self-invalidating by `test_the_exemption_is_not_stale`; item 4 gained the runtime guard beyond the spec's comment-and-test; and item 3's measured engine cost is ten searches where the serial version would have made nine. This spec **establishes no invariant**: items 1-2 bring `generate_recap_pdf.py` into conformance with the existing INV-111 and INV-184, item 4 documents and now tests the precondition behind INV-122's per-tab settle budget, and items 3 and 5 add no guarantee not already implied. Full suite: **1084 passed, 3 skipped, 561 subtests** (was 1070).
- **Commit:** e54fcf8

## deep-dive-audit-2026-07-30b

- **Implemented:** 2026-07-30
- **Not a spec** — a maintainer-requested full-plugin audit ("does the plugin conform to every invariant, and is it consistent, coherent and complete"), recorded here directly following the `deep-dive-audit-2026-07-27` / `-28` / `-28b` / `-30` precedent, because it changed shipped code and would otherwise have no record.
- **Files changed:** `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/module-07-query-visualize-discover/phase1-query-visualize.md`, `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/graduation/SKILL.md`, `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/scripts/capture_screenshots.py`, `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/bootcamp-onboarding/module-completion.md`, `docs/README.md`, `tests/test_bundled_script_and_production_paths.py` (new), `tests/test_tab_set_is_singular.py`
- **MCP re-check:** server **1.32.2**, 2026-07-30 — **n/a (no Senzing fact).** Nothing in this pass asserts anything about Senzing: a script path, a copy destination, two emitted strings, and an install command are all plugin- or harness-internal. The one external fact written — the Claude Code CLI's per-platform install commands — was verified against the vendor's own current documentation (code.claude.com/docs/en/setup, 2026-07-30) rather than recalled, and `claude --help` was run to confirm `--model`, `--effort` and `--permission-mode auto` are all real flags before leaving `docs/README.md`'s example command as written.
- **Invariants established: `INV-185` – `INV-189`** — resolved bundled-script paths; a handover copy preserving the project-relative path of verbatim-copied code; disclosure of an input the handover deliberately excludes; emitted strings bound by the same content rules as prose and verified by execution; install instructions covering every INV-001 platform.
- **Summary — four defects, none of which any of the 1059 existing tests could see, because each is a string that is wrong only relative to a working directory or a runtime the tests did not model.** **(1) Module 7 invoked the discoveries generator by a bare project-relative path** — `python3 scripts/generate_discoveries_pdf.py`. It ships inside the plugin while the command runs in the Bootcamper's project, and INV-050 gives the project no top-level `scripts/`; reproduced from a project root as exit 2, `can't open file`. It was the **sole** invocation of that generator, and its step reads "report exactly what failed and continue", so the realistic outcome was the always-produced data-discoveries deliverable being reported missing. Now resolved via `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` with the documented skill-relative fallback; **both forms executed** against a six-section fixture — `PDF generated:`, exit 0, 96% retention. (INV-185) **(2) Graduation flattened `data/senzing-ready/**` into `production/data/`** while copying `src/load/**` verbatim, so the handover project's loader pointed at a directory that did not exist (INV-084). Destination corrected to `production/data/senzing-ready/`; the code is deliberately **not** rewritten, since a path edited by graduation is a change the Bootcamper never saw made. (INV-186) The same table's exclusion of `data/raw/` leaves a CORD fast-pathed source's loader with no input at all — correct by design, now required to be named in the graduation report's files-excluded table and `production/README.md`. (INV-187) **(3) `capture_screenshots.py`'s `--help` and unknown-id error still enumerated the two removed tabs as "Known"**, eight ids for an app INV-155 fixes at six. `viz-reference-help-text-names-removed-tabs` (2026-07-29) fixed this class in the sibling script and extended the guard to this file — but the guard scans comments and docstrings, and these two strings are f-strings interpolated from `TABS` at runtime, so the "⛔ RESERVED" framing above the dict never travelled with the value. `TABS` still accepts both ids (an eight-tab snapshot must keep its slugs) and now says so on stderr when one is used. (INV-188) **(4) `docs/README.md` installed the Claude Code CLI "on macOS or Linux"** with no Windows route and no redirect, while INV-001 makes Windows supported and INV-158 makes the CLI one of the two interfaces the install docs must document. (INV-189) **One wording fix, no invariant:** `module-completion.md`'s summary backfill told the reader to "copy the Step 3 epilog you just showed the bootcamper" — but Module 0 is the one module that runs this step while exempt from Step 3 (INV-078/INV-092), so there was no epilog to copy and the three labeled blocks INV-157 requires had no stated source; the carve-out is now named inline. **Verified clean and worth not re-checking:** all 189 invariant IDs unique with no gaps and every `INV-NNN` citation repo-wide resolving; every spec but `todo.md` ledgered; the four hook scripts with **zero** test coverage (`session-start`, `session-end`, `precompact-recap`, `docker_lifecycle`) exercised in a sandbox — the recap fold is idempotent and append-only across repeated SessionStart/SessionEnd runs, and the Docker path degrades correctly; `--check` correctly flags an unfinalized `RECAP-CHECKPOINT` block, warns, and still renders at exit 0; the example recap PDF regenerates **byte-identically** apart from fpdf2's `/CreationDate` and the `/ID` derived from it (all 8 differing runs located and confirmed to be metadata only — INV-065); `propagate.sh` excludes `__pycache__`/`.pytest_cache` and the only surviving `docktermj` strings are the deliberate free-data repo and the certificate handle examples; every documented script flag exists; no live `data/transformed`, `Module 8`, "the Claude app" or `model_guidance` usage. **A false positive worth recording:** an anchor sweep reported `#env-script-path-resolution` and `#overlap-preserving-sampling` as broken across five files — both are `<a id=…>` tags, which a heading-slug checker cannot see. **14 new tests, each mutation-tested** (5 mutations, all caught); one of the new assertions was wrong before it shipped — a proximity window round the fast-pathed disclosure was too tight for wrapped prose and failed on correct text, now asserted per paragraph, and the failure was initially missed because a truncated preview hid the summary line. Suite: **1073 passed, 1 skipped** (was 1059), under both `pytest` and the INV-108 runner `python3 -m unittest discover -s tests`.
- **Commit:** `77cdd00` (the fixes and tests); this ledger entry and INV-185–INV-189 follow separately.

## deep-dive-audit-2026-07-30

- **Implemented:** 2026-07-30
- **Not a spec** — a maintainer-requested pass with two halves: (a) runtime-verify the three items the seven preceding audit specs could only *disclose*, and (b) sweep those same seven changes for the defect classes they were fixing in other people's work. Recorded here directly, following the `deep-dive-audit-2026-07-27` / `-28` / `-28b` precedent, because it changed shipped code and would otherwise have no record at all — a behaviour change with no ledger entry is the failure adjacent to the one INV-182 exists to prevent.
- **Files changed:** `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/scripts/senzing_viz_server.py`, `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/scripts/generate_discoveries_pdf.py`, `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/graduation/SKILL.md`, `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/module-03b-truthset-visualization/visualization-api-reference.md`, `tests/test_snapshot_and_capture_fidelity.py`, `tests/test_brand_sync.py`
- **MCP re-check:** server **1.32.2**, 2026-07-30 — **n/a (no Senzing fact).** Tool: `get_capabilities` (recorded first). Nothing in this pass asserts anything about Senzing: HTML escaping, a Markdown list marker, a brand-palette fallback and a Markdown normalization pass are all plugin-internal.
- **Invariant established: `INV-184`** (maintainer-approved 2026-07-30) — every shipped generator that inlines a fallback brand palette keeps it equal to `brand_tokens.py`, in one named constant, asserted by `tests/test_brand_sync.py`.
- **Summary — (a) the three disclosures are now runtime-verified and can be retired.** The Step 5a `--docs-dir production` pass was run against a purpose-built `production/` tree: **3 of 3** files normalized, exit 0, `production/src/` and `.env.example` untouched (md5), and a per-line content diff showing **exactly one** change across all three files — ` ``` ` → ` ```text `, the single exception the content guard documents (MD040). A Module 5 quality page built by following the guidance verbatim is offline (**zero** external references, vendored D3 inlined), branded (`brand_tokens` imports standalone and exposes every token named), and neutralises a `</script><script>alert('xss')</script>` field name in both contexts — `&lt;` in the table body, `\u003c` in the inline payload, 2 closing `</script>` tags, value round-tripping intact. `_script_json`/`_esc_html` hold against `</script>`, `</SCRIPT >`, `&`/`<b>` and U+2028/U+2029. What remains unverifiable without a bootcamp run is whether the *model* follows the guidance — `dry-run` phase 3, not a test. **(b) The sweep found three gaps, one of them introduced by this session.** (1) `graduation/SKILL.md` Pre-checks used `1a.` at column 0 inside an ordered list; `1a.` is not a CommonMark ordered-list marker, so it rendered as a paragraph that closed the list and split one block into three — now an indented `**1a — …**` continuation, with both `pre-check 1a` cross-references still resolving, and the session's whole diff swept for other instances (none). `tests/test_markdown_hygiene.py` cannot catch this class: it is deliberately scoped to space-before-comma and trailing whitespace. (2) `_esc_html` escaped only `& < >` while the visualization contract's "Two contexts, two rules" has always promised "(and quotes in attribute position)" — every call site is a text node so nothing rendered wrong, but yesterday's Module 5 guidance also names the attribute context, so the next caller could reasonably have reached for it and been wrong with no symptom (the INV-164 pattern: reference diverging from written rule). It now escapes both quote characters, carries a docstring stating which contexts it covers, and the contract's reference-implementation note tells any-language implementers to cover quotes. (3) `generate_discoveries_pdf.py`'s inlined fallback palette was unguarded — INV-107 names only the other two generators — and its own comment already claimed `tests/test_brand_sync.py` asserted it, which was false; the four triples were also duplicated across two `except` branches. Now one named `_FALLBACK_RGB`, asserted, with both distinct stderr messages preserved (INV-111). **Sweep found clean:** all 50 `INV-NNN` citations added this session resolve to defined invariants; every repo path cited exists bar three predicted-but-unused test filenames, each explained in its own ledger entry; no new guard skips (the suite's single skip is pre-existing and self-disclosing — `zsh` absent); no softened "should follow the Senzing" wording survives; INV-183 defers to INV-080 rather than contradicting it. **Two of the new assertions were wrong before they shipped**, both caught by mutation testing and both recorded in the tests as cautions: one accepted a key mentioned anywhere in Pre-checks rather than declared in its table, and one stripped the HTML escapes then hunted for the payload — which fails on *correct* output, since the payload is legitimately present as inert attribute data; only a parser distinguishes "no second attribute created" from "the string is gone". 11 new tests, each mutation-tested. Suite: **1059 passed, 1 skipped** (was 1053).
- **Commit:** `68629b7` (the code and tests); this ledger entry and INV-184 follow separately.

## refresh-example-recap-to-the-consolidated-app

- **Implemented:** 2026-07-30
- **Files changed:** `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/docs/examples/bootcamp_recap.example.md`, `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/docs/examples/bootcamp_recap.example.pdf` (re-rendered), `tests/test_example_recap_sync.py`. The spec also predicted `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/docs/examples/bootcamp_recap.example.*.png` — five new sanitized captures — but marked them "**under Route A only**", and Route B was chosen, so no PNG was added and the single existing capture is unchanged.
- **MCP re-check:** server **1.32.2**, confirmed current via `get_capabilities` earlier in this session (recorded first per Step 3.3) — **n/a (no Senzing fact).** The example is the plugin's own sanitized fixture; the endpoint count and tab set are the plugin's own contract, and no Senzing fact was written into the plugin by this change.
- **Establishes no new invariant.** It removes a false claim from a fixture. INV-146/INV-147 (embed every captured tab, in tab order) and INV-123 (captions derived from the opened image) already govern what it was getting wrong; INV-065 already requires the pair stay regenerable.
- **Summary:** **All 7 acceptance criteria hold, all runtime-verified, none deferred.** **Route B** (maintainer decision, 2026-07-30): the per-tab capture claim now discloses what the sanitized example actually ships — "(This sanitized example ships only the Entity Graph capture, below, to keep the plugin small — a real recap carries one image per captured tab, all of them, in tab order.)" — so the false claim is gone and the real rule is still taught. Route A was declined because inventing five screenshots would breach INV-123 as surely as the false claim did. The hardcoded endpoint count is gone from **three** places (the spec cited one) and replaced with count-free phrasing. `**Plugin version:**` now matches `plugin.json` (0.5.0), with an HTML note recording that it tracks the manifest and that a test asserts the match. Guards mutation-tested (key rename reverted, table rows dropped, missing-key branch removed) and reverted. Suite: 1039 passed, 1 skipped.

  place while the generator reads another is indistinguishable from never having asked". The

- **Invariant established: `INV-180`** (fold on `(data_source, record_id)` before counting; Senzing's resolved view, not the file, is the authority on an entity's record count).
- **Summary:** Extended "Iterate over records, not entity IDs" with a ⛔ block stating that one file row is not one record: the record identity is the caller-supplied pair, re-sending it replaces rather than accumulates, a mapped file may legitimately hold more rows than distinct keys when Module 5 chose to keep verified duplicate rows, and any program walking those rows must fold on the pair **before** counting or grouping — stated as a key-fold with no language idiom ("use whatever set/map your chosen language provides"). Named the real consequence (two entities reported as 23 and 15 records against 2 each, both falsely routed to over-matching review) and added the cross-check against Senzing's own resolved view. All five acceptance criteria hold. **Deviation recorded:** one addition beyond the spec — the cross-check also offers `RECORD_SUMMARY[]`'s `RECORD_COUNT`, since it is populated by the default flags on both search and find_network and so costs no extra call, composing with the sibling spec implemented in the same pass. The cited entity counts remain the reporting run's observation, quoted as such rather than re-verified, since reproducing them needs a live engine with that source loaded. Full suite: 964 passed, 1 skipped, 504 subtests.

## generators-warn-on-dropped-unencodable-characters

- **Implemented:** 2026-07-29
- **Files changed:** `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/scripts/generate_recap_pdf.py`, `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/scripts/generate_discoveries_pdf.py`, `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/module-07-query-visualize-discover/phase1-query-visualize.md`, `tests/test_recap_pdf_font_safety.py`
- **MCP re-check:** n/a (no Senzing fact — the Latin-1 limitation is the fpdf2 core fonts' and the drop is the plugin's own sanitisation). Server **1.32.2** confirmed current this session via `get_capabilities`, 2026-07-29. Verified against the working-tree generators instead.
- **Invariant established: `INV-178`** (a generator that drops an unencodable character must report it on stderr, ASCII-only, by Unicode name, with a locating excerpt, while still shipping the artifact).
- **Summary:** Closed the warn half of `_safe()`'s own documented "drops, warns" contract, which existed for the certificate name only. Added a `_DROPPED_CHARACTERS` collector plus `dropped_character_warning()` / `reset_dropped_characters()` to the shared sanitisation path in `generate_recap_pdf.py`, instrumented the one `_fold_to_latin1` branch that loses content outright, and emitted one aggregated `WARNING:` from both generators' success paths (`generate_discoveries_pdf.py` imports the reporter alongside `_safe`). Added authoring guidance at `phase1-query-visualize.md` telling the author to use each entity's **verified** Latin-script name or alias and ASCII diagram connectors, with an explicit prohibition on transliterating or inventing an unconfirmed name (INV-065's principle). **All seven acceptance criteria hold and are runtime-verified; none deferred.** The spec's own reproduction now emits `WARNING: 25 distinct character(s) in 3 passage(s) … CYRILLIC CAPITAL LETTER A, … BOX DRAWINGS …` with a backslash-escaped locating excerpt, still prints `PDF generated:` at `content retained: 96%`, and still exits 0 — retention figure and exit semantics unchanged, `?` substitution still forbidden, drop policy untouched. 11 new tests (964 passed, 1 skipped, 504 subtests) cover both generators, fenced blocks, table cells, the no-false-positive case, the shipped example recap, ASCII-only output, no raw echo of a dropped character, idempotence across render passes, and the locating excerpt. Both halves mutation-tested and reverted: removing the recording call and removing the discoveries emit are each caught by the intended tests. **Deviations recorded:** only the truly-lossy `except UnicodeEncodeError` branch is instrumented (instrumenting the combining-mark strip or `_LATIN_FOLD` would fire on every accented European name and break the no-false-positives criterion — `José Mařík āōş Zoë` → `Jose Marik aos Zoe` emits nothing); "the count" is distinct characters and passages rather than occurrences, because fpdf2 renders in two passes and may fall back, so an occurrence counter would report 2-3× the real loss; both generators' tests live in `test_recap_pdf_font_safety.py` since the mechanism is one shared collector; and the certificate-name and body warnings may both fire for a non-Latin name, unsuppressed on purpose. A wording bug was caught pre-ship: the first version put an em dash in the warning, contradicting its own ASCII guarantee.
- **Commit:** `2efef35`
