## recap-new-line-labels-regression-tests

- **Implemented:** 2026-07-29
- **Files changed:** `tests/test_new_line_labels.py` (new)
- **MCP re-check:** n/a (no Senzing fact — plugin PDF rendering only). Server **1.32.2** confirmed current this session via `get_capabilities`, 2026-07-29.
- **Establishes no new invariant.** The spec adds no behavior: it pins behavior that already shipped, and the rule it protects ("a list-shaped summary block is authored as a list") is the guarantee of its sibling spec `recap-summary-blocks-authored-as-bullets`, recorded there rather than duplicated here. A "every `_NEW_LINE_LABELS` entry must have a test" rule would bind one constant in two files rather than state a durable property of the plugin.
- **Summary:** Tests-only spec: the `_NEW_LINE_LABELS` label/gap/indent behavior was already implemented in both generators (uncommitted) and asserted by nothing. Added `tests/test_new_line_labels.py` — 11 tests, 7 subtests — holding both generators to one shared relative-layout contract via a `LabelLayoutAssertions` base: the allowlisted labels (`Why it matters:`; `Near-miss (the one that teaches more):`, `Measurement:`) put their value on a later line, indented, while a non-allowlisted label (`What we did:`, `Cross-source overlap:`) stays inline — pinning selectivity, which is what makes the allowlist load-bearing. Layout is asserted relatively (later line, greater x), never against the millimetre constants. Allowlist keys are asserted to match their labels **through each generator's own normalizer** (`_normalize_heading`, `_normalize`) and to already be in normal form, which is the regression that would otherwise be invisible. Text position is read from the PDF content stream by a page-tree-aware extractor, so `pdftotext` is not a dependency (per `specs/pdf-layout-verification-without-poppler.md`); per-page grouping makes the y comparisons sound. All seven acceptance criteria hold, including the two named existing tests (`test_consecutive_paragraphs_have_a_blank_line_between_them`, `test_a_soft_wrapped_label_is_not_split_mid_sentence`), which still pass. No generator was modified. Each guard was mutation-tested and reverted: emptying the recap allowlist, rewriting a discoveries key to its visible non-normalized form, and dropping the `not force_new_line` guard from both generators — all three caught, by the intended tests. Full suite: 953 passed, 1 skipped, 490 subtests.
- **Commit:** `3bbea69`

## dry-run-2026-07-29-phases-1-and-2

- **Implemented:** 2026-07-29
- **Files changed:** `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/scripts/generate_recap_pdf.py`, `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/module-03b-truthset-visualization/phase1-visualization.md`, `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/module-03b-truthset-visualization/SKILL.md`, `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/module-03b-truthset-visualization/visualization-api-reference.md`, `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/module-07-query-visualize-discover/phase2b-discover.md`, `specs/INVARIANTS.md`, `tests/test_recap_measure_font_safety.py` (new), `tests/test_truthset_acquisition_call.py` (new), `tests/test_partial_row_and_schema_coverage.py`
- **Not a spec** — a `/dry-run` the maintainer requested directly, phases 1 and 2, then asked to have all four findings fixed. Phase 3 (conversational) deferred to run against the repaired plugin. 909 tests were green before it and none of the four findings was mechanically enforced.
- **MCP re-check:** server **1.32.1**, 2026-07-29. Every `action=`/`topic=`/`category=`/`workflow=` literal in the plugin (24 distinct) validated against the live schemas; `get_sample_data`, `get_sdk_reference`, `find_examples`, `mapping_workflow`, `analyze_record`, `generate_scaffold`, `download_resource`, `reporting_guide` schemas loaded and diffed against every calling file. Two of the four findings came from this: see P1-1 and P1-2.
- **Establishes no new invariant.** P2-1/P2-2 are ordinary defects under INV-143/INV-157; P1-1 is an INV-136 violation; P1-2 is a **correction to INV-149 in place** (dated note, per the dry-run skill's rule 5 — an invariant encoding a false premise is worse than a missing one).
- **P1-1 (High): the Truth Set acquisition step made a call that cannot succeed, and its fallback trigger was stale.** `phase1-visualization.md` Step 1.1 said "Call `get_sample_data` and inspect the response" — but `dataset` is **required** ("schema-respecting clients cannot omit it"), so the call fails and reports nothing about availability (INV-136). The classification compounded it: `unavailable` = "only the CORD collections (Las Vegas, London, Moscow) are present", while `dataset='list'` on 1.32.1 returns **four** datasets, the fourth being `truthset` with `available: true` (159 records across CUSTOMERS/REFERENCE/WATCHLIST — the codes and count the plugin's own example recap cites). `dataset='truthset'` appeared **nowhere in the plugin**. So the module was written to conclude the MCP primary path had failed when it works, dropping to `github_fallback` and recording the wrong provenance — against the step's own declaration that MCP "always takes precedence", and putting INV-077's guarantee on the fallback. Both files now name `dataset='list'` for discovery and `dataset='truthset'` for retrieval, decide availability from `available_datasets`, describe the fallback as exceptional, and take codes and counts **from the response, never from the file** — the counts are deliberately *not* asserted in the test, since hardcoding them is how the old classification went stale.
- **P1-2 (Medium-High): INV-149's first verified claim was false, and a test pinned it.** `get_sdk_reference(topic='response_schemas', filter='find_network')` **now documents the element fields** — `ENTITY_NETWORK_LINKS[].MIN_ENTITY_ID`, `.MAX_ENTITY_ID`, `.MATCH_KEY`, `.MATCH_LEVEL_CODE`, `.ERRULE_CODE`, `.IS_AMBIGUOUS`, `.IS_DISCLOSED`, plus `ENTITIES[].RESOLVED_ENTITY.*` and `ENTITY_PATHS[].*` — where INV-149 recorded "with **no** element fields" as verified 2026-07-26. The contract shipped the negative claim "**NOT in `response_schemas`**", and `test_partial_row_and_schema_coverage.py` *required* it plus that the keys be marked unverified — so the suite held a false premise in place and forced the plugin to understate what MCP confirms. INV-149 corrected in place with a dated note; its **second** half still holds (`get_version` returns `data: []`), and the rule it states is unchanged. The contract and `phase2b-discover.md` now cite `response_schemas` as the authority with the 2026-07-28 dump as corroboration, and **still require the lookup and the dump** — the standing of the names changed, not the discipline. Five assertions in the test were reversed or rewritten, each carrying why.
- **P2-1 (High): the recap PDF fell back to the stdlib renderer on any unfinalized recap.** With fpdf2 2.8.5 installed the generator reported `renderer: stdlib` — a defect, not a fallback — because `fpdf2 render failed: Character "—" … outside the range … "helvetica"`. Traceback: `_render_certificate` → `_cert_module_layout` → `_wrap_to_width` → `_cert_text_width` → `pdf.get_string_width`. **fpdf2's `get_string_width` runs the same `normalize_text` as its text writers**, so measurement raises on exactly the characters `_safe` folds — and every *write* went through `_safe` while **none of the five measurements did**. Cost: the keepsake loses real tables (INV-142) and the branded certificate (INV-156), at exit 0, signalled only by one word. `test_recap_pdf_font_safety.py` passed throughout because it covers writes. Fixed as a **class**, per the skill's rule 1: a single `_width(pdf, text)` helper folds before measuring and all five sites now route through it, so no future call site can reintroduce the hazard — and the folded string is also the *correct* width, since it is what gets drawn.
- **P2-2 (Medium) and P2-1's trigger share one root cause: `_split_title_date`.** It split `## Name — <date>` only when the tail began with four digits, so the `— in progress` suffix the durability hooks leave on a folded-but-unfinalized section (INV-059) stayed glued to the title. That single stale title produced both symptoms: the certificate joins and fits module titles, carrying the em dash into a measurement; and `--check --expect-modules` compares against the title, so a present section read as absent — one run reported the same section as both validated-by-name and *"has no recap section at all"*, which sends graduation to backfill a section that already exists (INV-157 warns against exactly that). Now recognizes the `in progress` marker the plugin itself produces and returns it in the date slot; deliberately narrower than loosening the date test, so a module name legitimately containing " — " is still not mangled.
- **Verification:** every fix negative-controlled by reintroducing the defect and confirming failure, then restoring byte-identically (skill rule 3, not optional). Reverting P2-1+P2-2 together reproduced the **verbatim** original symptom (`renderer: stdlib` plus the em-dash message) and failed 6 tests; P1-1's revert failed 9; P1-2's failed 1. 937 tests pass (from 909): `test_recap_measure_font_safety.py` adds 14, `test_truthset_acquisition_call.py` adds 14.
- **Verified correct (so the next audit need not re-check):** all six hooks silent and exit 0 outside a bootcamp; the write-gate blocks `/tmp`, `~/Downloads`, `C:\Windows\Temp`, PEM, AWS key id and `AQAAAD` blob while allowing project paths **including a project whose own path contains `tmp`**; feedback-capture injects on feedback and verbosity prompts and stays silent otherwise; stop-nudge silent on every bias-to-silence condition and both INV-055 opt-outs; **INV-059's fold is exactly idempotent** (folds on run 1, byte-stable on runs 2 and 3, completed section's four subsections intact); the feedback file is byte-identical through the normalizer (INV-067); the discoveries generator refuses junk and writes no PDF (INV-110); an unknown tab id is rejected by name with no file saved under it (INV-122); `color_for_sources` gives 9 distinct order-independent encodings for 9 non-Truth-Set sources (INV-127); the viz server writes **no** snapshot when the engine is unavailable, in both failure modes (INV-077's blank-page risk); **zero** hits from the server's own `common_confabulations` list; INV-132's in-place correction still accurate; INV-160 reproduces exactly, including the response advertising an undeclared `inline` parameter it forbids adopting; `reporting_guide`'s two unnamed topics omitted deliberately with documented reasons. The live library also reproduced `SENZ7426|Transliteration failed`, corroborating the SUPPORTPATH spec rewritten 2026-07-28.
- **Coverage limits (explicit).** **No records were ever loaded and no SDK-dependent path was exercised.** `libSz.so` loads with `LD_LIBRARY_PATH=/opt/senzing/er/lib` and `senzing_core` imports, but engine initialization fails at `SENZ7426` because the support data is not configured — so INV-151 (redo drain termination), INV-152 (factory lifetime), INV-083/INV-089 (register-before-load) and any real resolution remain unverified, exactly as in previous runs. zsh is still absent, so INV-175's zsh branch is again untested. Chrome capture never ran: `capture_screenshots.py` validated tab ids and exited first. **Phase 3 (conversational — INV-005–INV-009, INV-012, INV-029–INV-032 apparatus and gate ordering) was not run** and remains the standing gap; it cannot be self-played.
- **Commit:** `7683d61`

## deep-dive-audit-2026-07-28b

- **Implemented:** 2026-07-28
- **Files changed:** `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/bootcamp-onboarding/module-completion.md`, `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/module-03b-truthset-visualization/phase2-close.md`, `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/docs/examples/bootcamp_recap.example.md`, `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/docs/examples/bootcamp_recap.example.pdf`, `tests/test_recap_pdf_images.py`, `tests/test_example_recap_sync.py`; then for finding 3 (2026-07-29) `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/scripts/senzing_viz_server.py`, `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/module-03b-truthset-visualization/visualization-api-reference.md`, `specs/INVARIANTS.md`, `tests/test_graph_label_distinctness.py`
- **Not a spec** — a fourth full-repo invariant-conformance and coherence audit the maintainer requested directly, then asked to have findings 1, 2 and 4 fixed in place. Distinct from `deep-dive-audit-2026-07-28` earlier the same day (that one established INV-159); recorded separately so the two audits' findings and file sets stay legible. 883 tests were green before it and none of the findings was mechanically enforced.
- **MCP re-check:** n/a (no Senzing fact — every finding is internal consistency between the plugin's own files and its own generator). Server 1.32.1, 2026-07-28.
- **Establishes no new invariant** — findings 1, 2 and 4 are **failures to apply INV-161/INV-162**, recorded the same day, to everything they bind; finding 3 is the same shape against **INV-153**. In every case the rule was already right and its retro-application was incomplete, so INV-153's scope was clarified in place rather than a new invariant recorded.
- **Finding 1 (High, live flow): every screenshot was being lost from every Bootcamper's recap PDF.** INV-161 moved image resolution to the recap document's own directory. The recap is `docs/bootcamp_recap.md` and its screenshots are `docs/visualizations/*.png`, so the only path that resolves is `visualizations/<file>.png`. `graduation/SKILL.md:473` said exactly that and explicitly forbade the alternative — but `module-completion.md:98` and `:199`, which write the recap at **every** module close, instructed `![caption](docs/visualizations/…)`, and `phase2-close.md:125` verified against the same broken form. Confirmed against the live resolver: the instructed path returns `None`, the graduation-documented one resolves. So the resolver was fixed and the instructions that *produce* the paths were updated in one place out of four; graduation's backfill could not recover it, because an embed that exists but points nowhere is not a *missed* embed. All three sites now carry the document-relative form **and the reason** (`docs/`-prefixed resolves to `docs/docs/…`), since a bare corrected example invites being "helpfully" corrected back.
- **Finding 2 (Medium-High, shipped artifact): the example recap was no longer reproducible.** `bootcamp_recap.example.md` referenced its screenshot as `docs/examples/…`, valid only under the pre-INV-161 cwd semantics: a fresh render gave `embedded 0 of 1 images` and 4 image objects against the committed 5, breaking INV-065's "MUST remain regenerable". The HTML comment above the path documented the outlawed behaviour as correct and conceded the path would not display in a Markdown reader — exactly what INV-161 forbids. Worse, `tests/test_example_recap_sync.py`'s docstring prescribed `cd plugins/senzing-bootcamp` before regenerating, which INV-161 names as forbidden, and its assertions passed only because they inspected the **committed** PDF whose image was baked in by an older render. Path made document-relative, comment rewritten, PDF regenerated from the repo root (5 images, byte-identical 162,910 bytes, `--check` clean from an unrelated cwd).
- **Finding 4 (Low): a stale claim contradicting INV-162.** `module-completion.md:98` said the PDF "silently skips" missing images; INV-162 requires each drop be named on stderr with an `embedded N of M images` count. Same sentence as Finding 1, corrected with it and now swept for across all three sites.
- **Finding 3 (Low-Medium, fixed 2026-07-29 after the maintainer settled the scope question): INV-153's distinctness rule was implemented for match keys only.** `senzing_viz_server.py:849` cut graph node labels at a fixed 20 characters with no collision check, so two organizations sharing a 19-character prefix — `ACME HOLDINGS INTERNATIONAL LLC` and `…INC` share 27 — rendered as the same string on the one surface whose purpose is telling entities apart, while `drawMatchKeys` compared its fitted labels and disambiguated. The blocker was a scope call only the maintainer could make, because the two answers have different mechanics: "it binds" permits clarifying INV-153 in place (`INVARIANTS.md:33`), while "graph labels are exempt" would be a change of meaning requiring a new ID (`:34`). The maintainer ruled **it binds** (2026-07-29). Fixed in three places, because the contract half is the load-bearing one: **(1)** `visualization-api-reference.md`'s "Defaults at production scale" section now leads with `**1. Every truncated label must stay distinguishable — whatever it labels**` as its own general requirement, with the match-key rule renumbered to 2 as its application and the graph default to 3 — a server generated in another language is built from that contract and never reads the Python, which is exactly how the `NAME_FULL` defect shipped (INV-164); **(2)** the reference now fits labels, compares the **fitted** strings, suffixes only a genuine collision (two entities that really share a name still render alike, per "unless their underlying values are identical"), and gives the label its own `<title>` rather than relying on the group tooltip; **(3)** `tests/test_graph_label_distinctness.py` adds 18 tests. INV-153's wording was clarified in place to state the scope explicitly — no meaning change, since the first clause was always unqualified and the match-key sentence was always an application of it.
- **Verification:** the new guards were proven to catch the defects by reintroducing both original forms — 6 failures — and restoring. 891 tests pass (from 883); `test_recap_pdf_images.py` adds a 6-test class asserting no skill instructs a `docs/`-prefixed embed, that the reason is stated, that a recap written as the skills instruct embeds 6 of 6, and that no site claims silent skipping; `test_example_recap_sync.py` adds a fresh-render class that renders from an unrelated cwd, since INV-065's "regenerable" is a property of a fresh render and cannot be established from committed bytes.
- **Method and its limits:** static analysis (link/anchor resolution, dangling `INV-NNN`, cross-module `Step N` references, layout-tree drift, hook exec form and purposes, tab-set and threshold agreement, model/effort table, MCP-grounding clauses, question forms) plus live execution probes of the recap generator. The conversational invariants — INV-005–INV-009, INV-012, INV-029–INV-032 gate ordering and apparatus — are out of reach of this method and still need a `dry-run`. Thirteen live invariants have no test naming them (INV-094, 101, 118, 144, 145, 150, 151, 152, 153, 157, 172 among them); of those examined, only INV-153 showed an actual gap.
- **Commit:** `a8ee925` (findings 1, 2, 4); `a27684a` (finding 3, 2026-07-29)

## env-script-must-be-shell-portable

- **Implemented:** 2026-07-28
- **Files changed:** `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/module-02-sdk-setup/SKILL.md`, `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/module-03-system-verification/phase1-verification.md`, `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/bootcamp-onboarding/ground-rules.md`, `specs/INVARIANTS.md`, `tests/test_env_script_shell_portability.py`, `specs/env-script-must-be-shell-portable.md` (deviation + invariant notes)
- **MCP re-check:** server **1.32.1**, 2026-07-28 — **changed, and the spec's own `n/a (no Senzing fact)` was wrong.** The spec asserts a Senzing fact: that a mis-resolved root surfaces as a JVM *"Unable to get settings"*, *"an error that points at the SDK"*. `search_docs(query='"Unable to get settings"')` finds that string **only** inside Senzing's own official snippets (`senzing/code-snippets-v4` — `java/snippets/information/GetVersion.java`, `GetLicense.java`, the C# `Program.cs` equivalents), in the sample program's *own* guard, which prints it and throws `IllegalArgumentException`/`ArgumentException`. `search_docs(category='troubleshooting')` and `explain_error_code('0058')` return no engine error of that name; the nearest codes, `SENZ0058 EAS_ERR_SZ_INITIALIZATION_FAILURE` and `SENZ0050 EAS_ERR_SZCORE_NOT_INITIALIZED`, are **not** documented as the symptom of an unset environment variable, so neither is cited (INV-169). `SENZING_ENGINE_CONFIGURATION_JSON` re-confirmed as the documented variable via `search_docs(category='configuration')`. Two changes to the fix followed: the guidance tells readers the symptom carries **no SENZ code** and must **not** be routed through `explain_error_code` — the reverse of the spec's framing — and, because the guard tests `settings == null` (*unset*, not empty), the snippet **refuses to export an empty configuration** rather than merely checking the path.
- **Summary:** Module 2's env-script rule now carries the anchored canonical statement (`<a id="env-script-path-resolution"></a>`) that a sourced script MUST resolve its own path in the platform's **default** shell, with a runnable bash/zsh idiom: branch on `${ZSH_VERSION}` to `${(%):-%x}`, else `${BASH_SOURCE[0]:-$0}`; verify the computed root holds `config/engine_config.json`; fail naming the resolved path; refuse an empty configuration; then export. `ground-rules.md` gains a "Sourced scripts and the default shell" section immediately after the Windows/PowerShell one so both supported platforms' shell semantics sit together, and it **links** the anchor rather than restating it. Module 2's Troubleshooting and Module 3's Step 1a both name the symptom and re-attribute it to the snippet guard, and Step 1a checks for a missing SENZ code **before** reaching for `explain_error_code`. **Three things beyond the spec:** (1) `return 1`, never `exit 1` — the spec mandates failing loudly, and the obvious way to fail in a sourced script closes the Bootcamper's terminal while `set -e` leaks into their session, so implementing item 3 literally would have traded a silent wrong root for a killed shell; (2) the shipped snippet is **executed** by the tests — extracted from the fenced block after the anchor, installed in a synthetic project tree and sourced — because a present-but-wrong idiom is precisely this defect; (3) verified by running it that bash **parses** the zsh-only `${(%):-%x}` in the untaken branch without a bad-substitution or syntax error, so no `eval` guard is needed. Scoped to bash and zsh, not POSIX `sh` (`${BASH_SOURCE[0]}` is a bad substitution under dash and no POSIX way exists), and Windows keeps `senzing-env.bat` with `%~dp0`. The same-shell requirement and the `.bat` path are untouched, asserted verbatim by a test. **NOT runtime-verified:** acceptance criterion 2's **zsh** half — zsh is not installed in this environment (`command -v zsh` returns nothing), so it is disclosed rather than ticked; `test_zsh_resolves_the_same_root_as_bash` exists, skips with that reason, and will run wherever zsh is present. The bash half, the fail-loud guard, the empty-config refusal, cwd-independence and shell survival are all runtime-verified by sourcing the snippet. 882 tests pass (from 851); `tests/test_env_script_shell_portability.py` adds 31 plus 1 skip.
- **Invariant:** `INV-175` (sourced scripts resolve their own path in the default shell; verify before exporting; `return`, never `exit`; never export an empty value).
- **Commit:** `32e9c9b`

## module6-validation-routing-not-reports-sql

- **Implemented:** 2026-07-28
- **Files changed:** `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/module-06-data-processing/SKILL.md`, `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/module-06-data-processing/phaseD-validation.md`, `tests/test_sampling_and_validation_routing.py`, `specs/module6-validation-routing-not-reports-sql.md` (deviation note)
- **MCP re-check:** server **1.32.1**, 2026-07-28 — the subject was already redirected at triage and that redirect was re-confirmed before quoting. `reporting_guide(topic='reports', scale='small')` states in its own `schema.description` that the `sz_dm_*` tables "are **NOT** part of the Senzing SDK and do **NOT** exist out of the box… must be created and maintained by a separate data mart replication pipeline that YOU build and operate", carries an anti-pattern that SQLite is POC-only, and includes `Validation:` SQL that runs against **exported entity JSON** rather than the data mart. So the server is candid and the defect is the plugin's routing.
- **Summary:** **Establishes no new invariant** — routing precision under existing rules (INV-117's no-SQL, INV-080's provenance). Module 6 routed counts and statistics to `reporting_guide` **without naming a topic** while being specific everywhere else, so `topic='reports'` is the name a reader picks for "counts and statistics" and its data-mart SQL dominates the reply. `phaseD-validation.md`'s header and `SKILL.md:63` now name `topic='evaluation'` for this phase's single-pass export statistics and `topic='export'` for extraction, and the step 22 count bullet names the topic too. A ⚠️ block characterizes `topic='reports'` rather than merely avoiding it — production reporting, not evaluation — **quoting the tool's own schema note verbatim with the server version and date** so nothing is asserted on the plugin's authority, and naming the `Validation:` patterns as the usable subset so "wrong topic" does not read as "nothing here helps". The no-SQL rule is unchanged. **Scope checked exhaustively rather than at the three cited lines:** an audit of every `reporting_guide` reference in Module 6 found the spec's three were the only untopiced ones; all nine now carry a topic, so the acceptance criterion holds for the module and not just the cited lines. **No upstream action:** the entry records `Upstream: submitted 2026-07-28`, and that submission rests on the premise this re-check refutes — Step 8 forbids re-filing, so it is recorded in the spec for the maintainer to decide on. All acceptance criteria met; nothing required a live engine.

## overlap-preserving-sampling-at-the-license-gate

- **Implemented:** 2026-07-28
- **Files changed:** `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/module-04-data-collection/SKILL.md`, `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/module-06-data-processing/phaseD-validation.md`, `tests/test_sampling_and_validation_routing.py`, `specs/overlap-preserving-sampling-at-the-license-gate.md` (deviation note)
- **MCP re-check:** n/a (no Senzing fact — the defect is guidance placement inside the plugin, and the overlap measurements are the reporter's observations of their own data, cited as such). Server 1.32.1, 2026-07-28.
- **Summary:** **Establishes no new invariant** — it relocates and sharpens existing guidance. Module 4 offered sampling at the licence gate with no strategy, so a random 300 records from each of five sources loaded flawlessly (1,147 records, zero errors, redo drained, quality 94–100%) and produced **zero cross-source matches**, while the full files held 507 shared names across 21,284 × 63,193 candidates for the largest pair. The correct guidance already existed at Step 8b, in the branch that fires only on SQLite load-time concerns — never on the path a bootcamper meets first. Step 6 now carries the **canonical** anchored sampling rule (`<a id="overlap-preserving-sampling"></a>`): random selection destroys the signal when 2+ sources are present, why profiling and entity resolution want opposite things, the green-signals warning, and a concrete four-step selection method (identify shared join keys → select records participating in 2+ sources, keeping matched groups whole → fill the remaining budget → record the strategy and its reason). The smaller-slice path and Step 8b **link** to it, and Step 8b is told not to restate it — a test counts the references, because this defect *was* two copies of one rule disagreeing. Module 6's step 23 now reads the recorded sampling method before treating a low cross-source count as a finding, names `config/data_sources.yaml` as where it lives, and forbids reporting "Senzing found no cross-source matches" without that check. **Three things beyond the spec:** (1) a third harmful site the spec did not identify — the module instructed *"Ensure the sample is representative of the full dataset"*, the harmful instinct as an instruction, now replaced and asserted gone by a test; (2) a single-source exemption, since the spec's unconditional rule would become a ritual on datasets with no cross-source overlap to preserve; (3) the Module 6 check landed first in step 22 (single-source UAT), where no cross-source count exists, and was moved to step 23 — a test pins it between the step 23 and 24 headings. All acceptance criteria met; nothing required a live engine.
- **Commit:** `66be335`

## record-preview-requires-registered-source

- **Implemented:** 2026-07-28
- **Files changed:** `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/module-05-data-quality-mapping/phase1-quality-assessment.md`, `tests/test_quality_score_and_preview_prereq.py`, `specs/record-preview-requires-registered-source.md` (deviation note)
- **MCP re-check:** server **1.32.1**, 2026-07-28 — **still reproduces.** `get_sdk_reference(topic='parameters', filter='getRecordPreview')` returns both overloads for every binding with **no** configuration prerequisite noted. `explain_error_code('SENZ2207')` confirms `EAS_ERR_DATA_SOURCE_CODE_DOES_NOT_EXIST` **and** returns actionable resolution steps (register the code, confirm the registered list, mind case-sensitivity, reinitialize) — the opposite of this session's `SENZ7221` case, which is why the plugin signposts rather than diagnoses.
- **Summary:** **Establishes no new invariant** — it signposts a method and its prerequisite under rules that already exist (INV-080 MCP-sourcing, INV-132 per-binding shapes, INV-048 non-blocking, INV-163's say-which-check-ran). `getRecordPreview` appeared **nowhere** in the plugin before this, so a bootcamper reaching for the module's most valuable verification — asking Senzing how it reads a record rather than eyeballing field names — got no warning that preview needs the record's `DATA_SOURCE` registered despite writing nothing. Phase 1's readiness step (`## 5a`) now introduces the method as a stronger check than the structural test, states the prerequisite with the verbatim `SENZ2207` symptom, gives the working order (register via `sdk_guide(topic='configure')`, then preview) and notes registration is idempotent and needed by the loading phase anyway so it costs nothing here. It routes `SENZ2207` through `explain_error_code` and **deliberately does not restate** its resolution steps, because a copy would drift from the server's. The check is optional and non-blocking, and must say which check ran so "Senzing-ready" never implies a stronger test than was performed. No per-binding signature ships — a test asserts the step contains no argument list (INV-132). **Deviation recorded:** the spec named step 5a as "the Senzing-readiness step", which is correct but **CORD-only** ("Non-CORD sources: skip this step entirely"), while the reporter hit `SENZ2207` on `ICIJ` — a non-CORD source. Rather than restructure the module, the prerequisite carries an explicit line that it applies wherever a preview-based check is used, not only to CORD sources, and a test asserts that sentence. Promoting preview to a general readiness step is its own spec. All acceptance criteria met; nothing required a live engine.
- **Commit:** `165a0fb`

## quality-score-per-record-type

- **Implemented:** 2026-07-28
- **Files changed:** `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/module-05-data-quality-mapping/phase1-quality-assessment.md`, `tests/test_quality_score_and_preview_prereq.py`, `specs/quality-score-per-record-type.md` (deviation note)
- **MCP re-check:** server **1.32.1**, 2026-07-28 — **confirmed and strengthened, and it constrained what shipped.** `search_docs(query='what features to map', category='data_mapping')` shows the Entity Specification marks type in its own wording: `DOB` is "Person date of birth", `NATIONALITY` / `CITIZENSHIP` / `PLACE_OF_BIRTH` are "Person …", `NAME` is listed separately for person and organization, and `REGISTRATION_DATE` / `REGISTRATION_COUNTRY` sit under section headings titled "(organizations)". So applicability is *derivable* from the specification rather than needing a hardcoded table — which is what the shipped guidance teaches. The search did **not** return labels for passport or gender, so those were not attributed.
- **Summary:** New invariant established: INV-174 (a completeness metric must not count against a record a field that cannot apply to it), extending INV-128 from what counts as a present *value* to what belongs in the *denominator*. Phase 1 step 6 defined presence rigorously and then averaged each feature's coverage across the whole source, so a feature inapplicable to most records dragged the score down exactly as if the data were missing: a sanctions list with NAME and ADDRESS on 100% of records scored 52% completeness / 69% overall — "recommend fixing before mapping" — because person-oriented features were averaged across a source where 71 of 110 records are ORGANIZATIONS. Per-record-type rescoring gave 97%, so the original figure would have sent the bootcamper to remediate clean data. The score is now defined per record against the features applicable to that record's `RECORD_TYPE`, with the method for deriving applicability (read the specification's description and section heading, via `search_docs`) rather than a static list, an illustrative table that **disclaims itself** as partial and re-readable, explicit handling for records with no `RECORD_TYPE` (score against kind-independent features, report the count, treat a mostly-untyped source as its own finding), and an extension to the existing uniformity sanity-check: a low score alongside high NAME/ADDRESS coverage is a probable applicability error, checked before anyone is routed to remediation. The presence rules are explicitly left intact. **Two deviations recorded:** (1) only the verified type labels were written into the plugin — the spec cites DOB, PASSPORT and GENDER, but the search confirmed only DOB among those, so the guidance names DOB with its verified label and refers to "others like passport and gender" without attributing a specification label (INV-080); (2) the worked case sits with the rule rather than beside the score-output example at `:229-235` as the spec suggested, because it concerns what belongs in the denominator, not output formatting. A third guard the spec did not anticipate: `ADDRESS`, `PHONE`, `EMAIL` and identifiers are named type-neutral so the correction cannot be over-applied and inflate every score. 27 tests in `tests/test_quality_score_and_preview_prereq.py`; full suite 825 passing. All acceptance criteria met; nothing required a live engine.
- **Commit:** `165a0fb`

## supportpath-failure-code-and-szproduct-masking

- **Implemented:** 2026-07-28
- **Files changed:** `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/module-02-sdk-setup/SKILL.md`, `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/module-03-system-verification/phase1-verification.md`, `tests/test_engine_verification_and_senz2027.py`
- **MCP re-check:** server **1.32.1**, 2026-07-28 — **the spec's original central claim was refuted, the spec was rewritten (`c05eb7c`), and this implements the corrected version.** `explain_error_code('SENZ7426')` → `EAS_ERR_XLITERATOR_FAILED: Transliteration failed`, causes listed as malformed input data, missing `DATA_SOURCE`/`RECORD_ID`, invalid JSON encoding — **nothing ties it to SUPPORTPATH**. `explain_error_code('SENZ2027')` → `EAS_ERR_PLUGIN_INIT: Plugin initialization error`, and `search_docs` returns the Senzing FAQ *"I get SENZ2027 Plugin initialization error GNR data files failed to load — You are missing the senzingsdk-runtime data directory. The libraries are present but the GNR data files (in `resources/data/`) are not deployed."* So `SENZ2027` **is** the documented missing-support-data symptom and `module-02-sdk-setup/SKILL.md`'s existing text was already correct. The per-class masking claim (`SzProduct` succeeds while every `SzEngine`/`SzDiagnostic` call fails) is stated by no MCP source and was **not** shipped.
- **Summary:** **Establishes no new invariant** — it hardens an existing verification step and records an MCP-sourced diagnostic under rules that already exist (INV-080 MCP-sourcing, INV-129 verify-the-thing, INV-150 detected-condition-governs). Module 2's Step 9 now **requires the check to create and use an `SzEngine` (or `SzDiagnostic`), not only `SzProduct`**, with the reason stated in the form the FAQ supports: the libraries and their support data can be present independently, so a version query proves the library loaded and not that the engine can initialize — which is how a wrong SUPPORTPATH passed verification and failed several steps later. Both success indicators were tightened so a version probe cannot satisfy them (the module header and the Success Criteria list). The code still comes from `generate_scaffold(workflow='initialize')`: the change constrains which class the generated check touches, not where the code comes from (INV-080), and a test asserts the no-hand-writing instruction survives. Step 8's `SENZ2027` mention now carries the FAQ's actionable finding — the runtime **data directory** is not where the configuration points, which on Windows/Scoop is exactly the sibling-directory case the existing `Test-Path` block fixes — quoted with its provenance, with `explain_error_code` still the first call. Module 3's phase 1 gained **Step 1a: Engine Initialization Check**, placed before synthetic-record generation, so a masked-through install cannot reach the load where the failure would read as a data problem; on failure it routes through `explain_error_code`, adds the `SENZ2027` cause that code's own resolution steps omit, and stops rather than proceeding. It is agent-side apparatus, not a 👉 question (INV-012). **The retraction is enforced, not just documented:** `tests/test_engine_verification_and_senz2027.py` scans every skill Markdown file and fails if `SENZ7426` appears within 300 characters of `SUPPORTPATH`, and separately fails if any file claims `SzProduct` keeps succeeding — so neither the refuted code claim nor the unverified masking claim can be reintroduced. 14 tests plus 34 subtests; full suite 773 passing. All acceptance criteria met; nothing here required a live engine, since every fact came from `explain_error_code`/`search_docs`.
- **Commit:** `06c33e9`

## verbatim-check-numeric-source-values

- **Implemented:** 2026-07-28
- **Files changed:** `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/module-05-data-quality-mapping/phase2-data-mapping.md`, `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/module-06-data-processing/phaseD-validation.md`, `tests/test_verbatim_check_limitation.py`, `specs/verbatim-check-numeric-source-values.md` (deviation note)
- **MCP re-check:** server **1.32.1**, 2026-07-28 — **confirmed, plus two facts the spec did not have.** Re-fetched the current `sz_verbatim_check.py` via the `download_resource` URL rather than trusting the report: `collect_strings()` still harvests under `isinstance(obj, str)` only, and the test is still whole-value membership (`if v.strip() not in allowed`). New: (a) an exemption hook exists but cannot help — `is_exempt(attr)` waives `EXEMPT_KEYS = {"DATA_SOURCE", "RECORD_ID"}` or any attribute ending `_TYPE`, so it is key-based and no *value* can be exempted, and `REL_POINTER_KEY` matches neither rule; (b) `search_docs(category='data_mapping')` shows the Entity Specification mandates no JSON type for the relationship keys — every `REL_POINTER_KEY` example is a string (`"ORG1001"`, `"ACME-1001"`) while the `REL_ANCHOR_KEY` guidance column shows a bare `1001`.
- **Summary:** New invariant established: INV-173 (a gate that cannot represent a legitimate input must not be treated as evidence about the data, must not block, and must not be satisfied by altering the data). The upstream defect was already reported (2026-07-28, `category='bug'`); this is the plugin-side mitigation that holds whether or not it is fixed there. `phase2-data-mapping.md` — where `mapping_workflow` step 4 runs the checker — now states that the gate is **unsatisfiable rather than strict** for a non-string source value, quoting the harvesting line, the membership test and the key-only waiver with their provenance (server 1.32.1, 2026-07-28); that **both** emissions are reported, so there is no passing form; and a four-step path: do not conclude the mapping is wrong, choose the emission on the Entity Specification's terms via `search_docs` rather than the checker's, record the exemption and its reason, proceed (INV-048). It forbids changing a source value to satisfy the tool **and says why that would not even work** — stringifying a numeric identifier still fails, because the allowed set was built without it. It also forbids shipping a forked copy of the checker, since the resource comes from the MCP server and a fork would mask the upstream fix (INV-080); a test asserts no copy exists in the plugin tree. `phaseD-validation.md` carries the caveat beside its existing "every earlier gate is static and structural" passage, so a violation list arriving unresolved in Module 6 is not misread as a mapping defect. **One of the spec's own claims was deliberately not shipped:** its `## Problem` records that resolving this needed a live-engine test confirming Senzing links disclosed relationships under both a string and a numeric pointer key — a live-engine observation absent from every MCP source, so writing it into the plugin would assert an unverified Senzing fact (INV-080). A test asserts that sentence is **not** present, so a future edit cannot quietly promote it. **One listed affected file needed no change:** `phase3-test-load.md` never references the verbatim check (its only "verbatim" is the unrelated "pinned verbatim"), so a caveat there would have invented a reference to a gate that step does not run. All acceptance criteria met — nothing here required a live engine. `tests/test_verbatim_check_limitation.py` adds 20 tests; full suite 759 passing.
- **Commit:** `52a9063`

## snapshot-port-and-dataset-wording

- **Implemented:** 2026-07-28
- **Files changed:** `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/scripts/senzing_viz_server.py`, `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/module-03b-truthset-visualization/visualization-api-reference.md`, `tests/test_snapshot_and_capture_fidelity.py`
- **MCP re-check:** n/a (no Senzing fact — both defects were string literals in a bundled script). Server 1.32.1, 2026-07-28.
- **Summary:** New invariant established: INV-172 (a retained artifact must not hardcode an environment fact nor assert a dataset identity the caller did not supply). `_snapshot_probe_html()` hardcoded `http://localhost:8080` and "this Truth Set" into text that ships into `docs/visualizations/*.html`, so a Module 7 snapshot on a non-default port told its reader to open a port nothing was listening on **and** mislabelled their own data as a Senzing demo set — silently, since the snapshot renders and the text is grammatical. Both values now thread through: `_snapshot_probe_html(model, engine, flags, port=…, dataset=…)` and `write_snapshot(..., port=…, dataset=…)` take them, `main()` passes the parsed `--port`, and a new `--dataset` option lets the Truth Set module say "the Senzing Truth Set" while Module 7 says e.g. "your CUSTOMERS data". The default is the neutral "the loaded data", so a future third caller cannot inherit a false label, and the label is passed through `_esc_html` because it reaches a retained HTML artifact (INV-106). Verified across all three caller shapes: `--port 9001` with no dataset yields "the loaded data" and no `8080`; a Truth Set caller still reads correctly; a Module 7 caller names the bootcamper's data. The requirement is stated in `visualization-api-reference.md` so a server generated in any language inherits it (INV-090/INV-124). 19 tests in `tests/test_snapshot_and_capture_fidelity.py` (shared with the capture spec below), including one asserting no `localhost:8080` literal remains in the note-building path and one asserting a `<script>` label is escaped. Full suite 739 passing.
- **Commit:** `ca890a1`

## default-tab-capture-without-injection

- **Implemented:** 2026-07-28
- **Files changed:** `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/scripts/senzing_viz_server.py`, `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/scripts/capture_screenshots.py`, `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/module-03b-truthset-visualization/visualization-api-reference.md`, `plugins/senzing-bootcamp/skills/bootcamp-onboarding/module-completion.md`, `tests/test_snapshot_and_capture_fidelity.py`, `specs/default-tab-capture-without-injection.md` (deviation note)
- **MCP re-check:** n/a (no Senzing fact — the defect is in a bundled capture script and the page it drives). Server 1.32.1, 2026-07-28.
- **Summary:** New invariant established: INV-171 (capture apparatus must not perturb what it captures; a view-switching function must be idempotent and animated content gets longer to settle). **Implemented differently from the spec, deliberately, and the deviation is recorded on the spec file.** The spec said to detect the page's default tab from the markup and skip the `activate()` injection for it. Two findings at implementation time made that wrong: (1) `buildNav()` builds the nav at runtime and marks index 0 active, so the served HTML contains no nav buttons and no active marker — a markup probe cannot work, and the spec's "fall back safely" branch would have been the only branch ever taken; (2) `applyDeepLink()` also calls `activate(tab)`, which is how the **live server** is captured, so a caller-side skip in `capture_screenshots.py` would have left that route still restarting the simulation. The guard therefore went into `activate()` itself — called for the tab already active it returns before `drawFor()` — which covers the injected route, the deep-link route and a user clicking the already-active nav button, and which lives in the any-language contract where a Python-side fix could not reach (INV-090/INV-124). Spec item 3 was implemented as written: `_virtual_time_ms(tab)` returns 30s for the force-directed tabs (`graph`, `network`) against the base 15s for static ones, threaded to the Chrome backend via module state rather than a new backend parameter, because `_BACKENDS` is invoked uniformly and an existing test substitutes two-argument callables for it. `module-completion.md` now tells the reader to open the Entity Graph image and check the nodes are spread, naming a much smaller file size as the tell. **One acceptance criterion is not runtime-verified:** the visual check (spread layout, file-size floor) needs a live server with loaded data this environment lacks — the mechanism is asserted instead (early return precedes `drawFor()`, contract requires idempotence, graph budget exceeds the static one). Full suite 739 passing, including the real-browser capture tests and the JS-executing viz tests.
- **Commit:** `ca890a1`

- **Summary:** New invariant established: INV-170 (a value the Bootcamper was asked for outranks anything auto-detected earlier, must be persisted everywhere the artifact is generated from, and a generator choosing between two sources must prefer the answer and report a disagreement). Graduation's pre-check correctly rejected the auto-detected handle `docktermj`, asked the pinned INV-113 question and persisted the answer as `name` in `config/bootcamp_preferences.yaml` — but `generate_recap_pdf.py` read the certificate name from the recap's `**Bootcamper:**` preamble line, written by Bootcamp preparation at the *start* of the run, so the certificate printed the rejected handle at exit 0 with 99% retention and no warning (INV-065). Fixed on both sides. The generator gained `read_preferences_name()` — a line-scanner matching `scripts/stop-nudge.py`'s precedent, so no third-party YAML parser is required (INV-052), tolerant of a missing/malformed file, a nested `name:`, quotes and inline comments — plus `set_certificate_name_override()` / `certificate_name()`, consulted by `_cert_fields()`, `recap_missing_certificate_name()` and `recap_certificate_name_unprintable()` so the name that is *judged* is the name that will *print*. Graduation's pre-check now persists to **both** places, with the reason stated (preferences alone leaves the recap showing the rejected handle, and a recap-driven re-render reproduces it) and a note that amending a preamble meta line is not forbidden by the append-only rule (INV-085). Verified with a real render: preferences `Dana Reyes` against recap `docktermj` prints **Dana Reyes** on the certificate page and not `docktermj`, confirmed per-page via `pdftotext` — the same probe class that originally caught the defect (INV-129). 19 tests in `tests/test_certificate_name_source.py`; `test_recap_pdf_certificate.py` and `test_recap_pdf_guard.py` pass unchanged.
